# AGENT_EN.md — NoteBudget (Android)

> **About this document.** This is the project's working log: the requirements, the numbered design decisions (D01 ...), the
> verification checklists and the history of the work, kept in English while the project was built together with AI assistants.
> It is long and reads as an internal document; the user-facing description is in the [README](../README.md) and
> [FUNCTIONALITY_RU.md](FUNCTIONALITY_RU.md). Mentions of `SIGNING_KEYS_RU.md` refer to the owner's private memo about the signing
> keys, which is deliberately **not** part of the repository. The application's name is **Notio by vibic**; "NoteBudget" is the
> original working name that still appears in the package name (`com.notebudget.app`, kept so updates install over old builds).

## 0. Purpose of this file

### 0.1 Goal

**The goal of the project is a working Android application NoteBudget**: it
builds, installs on a device/emulator (minSdk 28), and implements the
requirements of this document, verified by tests (see §15 and §17).

This document is the single source of truth for the coding agent that will
implement the application **from scratch**.

### 0.2 Truth about the current state

**State (latest), version 0.9.101 (build 118, 2026-10-09): D343 — the status of a task is its icon alone, the priority a bare field, the chips of the filter icons; their words are hints that lead into the help. Everything below (sections 1–20) is a **specification of target behavior**.
Wording such as "the app supports…", "is stored…", "is created…" means "must
support / must store / must create". It never means that the thing already
exists. The real state of the work is tracked **only** in:

- **§21 Progress tracker** — status of every phase and task;
- **§22 Work log** — dated record of what was actually done.

If the spec and §21 disagree about what exists, §21 (verified against the real
repository) wins.

### 0.3 Contents

The document contains:

- product requirements;
- accepted decisions;
- data invariants;
- data model and DB schema;
- architectural guidance;
- agent behavior rules;
- implementation order;
- readiness criteria;
- known gaps in the specification (§18.1);
- progress tracker and work log (§21, §22).

If something is marked `PENDING` or `TBD`, the agent **must not invent final
behavior on its own**. It must ask the user **one** question and wait for an
answer.

---

## 1. Agent behavior rules

### 1.1 Core protocol

1. This file is the source of truth for the product.
2. If the user gives a new requirement, the agent first records it in the
   `Decision Log` of this file, then implements it.
3. If a requirement is ambiguous, the agent asks **strictly one question at a
   time**.
4. Each question must contain:
   - a short summary;
   - answer options;
   - a recommended option;
   - an option to give a custom answer.
5. After receiving an answer, the agent must:
   - record the decision in the `Decision Log`;
   - update the relevant functional sections;
   - change the question status from `PENDING` to `ACCEPTED`;
   - bump the document version if needed.
6. Do not implement a disputed feature if its behavior breaks data integrity or
   security.
7. Any deletion in the app must be confirmed by the user.
8. All user-facing strings must be localizable.
9. Secrets, passwords, pattern locks, and biometric data must not be stored in
   plaintext.
10. **Progress tracking is mandatory.** After every completed task the agent
    updates this file in the same working step:
    - tick the item in §21 (`[ ]` → `[x]`) and update the phase status;
    - append a dated line to §22 (what was done, which files, how it was
      verified);
    - update `current_phase` and `last_updated` in the front matter.
11. **Never claim a task is done without evidence** (build passes, test run,
    file exists). If it was not verified, it stays `[ ]` or gets a note.
12. If reality differs from this document (a decision changed, a gap was found),
    fix the document first — do not silently deviate.
13. **Keep the sibling-platform docs current (user, 2026-09-22; rewritten the same day, D205; paths updated 2026-09-24).** This is the
    flagship app; the desktop apps are separate projects outside this repository (see §2.1): `D:\Notio-Windows` (started,
    real code since 2026-09-24) and `D:\Notio-Linux` (planning only so far). **There is no iOS project any
    more:** the user removed `Notio-iOS` on 2026-09-22, so nothing here refers to it or updates it. Whenever a Decision
    Log entry here **adds or changes a user-facing feature** (not an internal refactor, not a bug fix that restores
    already-decided behaviour), the **same working step** also:
    - adds a row to the "Feature parity with Android" table (§5) of **both** `D:\Notio-Windows\AGENT.md` and
      `D:\Notio-Linux\AGENT.md`: the Android decision ID, one line of what it is, the Android version, and `PLANNED`;
    - updates their `last_updated` and the "Android version this file is in step with" line at the top of §5;
    - touches their README only when the product description there stops being true.
    A one- or two-line pointer back to the Android decision ID is enough; nothing needs to be designed for the desktop
    yet. The purpose: nothing Android ships is a surprise when the desktop implementation phase starts. At the end of a
    working step the agent checks that the two tables end at the same Android decision ID as this Decision Log. The
    other direction (desktop -> Android, and Windows <-> Linux) is rule 15.
14. **Cloud services are on pause (user, 2026-09-24: "rule: we do not touch cloud systems for now, postponed, on pause").**
    No work on Google Drive, Yandex Disk or any other cloud (sign-in, sync, cloud backups, cloud SDKs) in this app or in
    `D:\Notio-Windows` / `D:\Notio-Linux` — not proposed, not planned, not started — until the user lifts the pause explicitly.
    Backups stay local files (export / import); the person may put such a file anywhere by hand. The history: D218 - D220,
    removed in D223, results in `docs/CLOUD_INTEGRATION_RU.md`, code in commit 4fbd00f.

15. **Parity rule revised (user, 2026-09-27: "new functions come from the Android version, desktop ones are marked desktop-only, and then they are synced with Notio Linux; if I explicitly ask to sync some function to Android, then add it to the Android version's TODO list; otherwise only to the Linux version").** This supersedes the directions of the rule below: (1) **Android -> desktop:** every user-facing function added, changed or improved on Android goes to Windows §5 and Linux §5 as before. (2) **Desktop-only functions** (window, tray, keyboard, single instance, installers …) are marked **DESKTOP-ONLY** in the Decision Log of the project that builds them and go **only to the other desktop project** (Windows -> Linux §5.1, Linux -> Windows §5.1); they are **not** sent to Android §2.2. (3) **To Android** a desktop function goes only when the user explicitly asks for it: then it is added to `D:\Notio-Android\docs\TODO_IDEAS_RU.md` (and its §2.2 row), nothing else.
    **Three-way feature parity (the main rule; user, 2026-09-24: "the main rule, in our project and in the Android one: every change of functions — adding, improving — is recorded in the root project and in the linked one, so that the linked project is brought up to the current state; if we build functions in the Windows version, the Android project must know about it and get the same functionality, and the other way round"; "the same for the Linux project, the Notio-Linux folder: a link between all three").** The three Notio projects — Android (`D:\Notio-Android`), Windows
    (`D:\Notio-Windows`), Linux (`D:\Notio-Linux`) — are one product. Whenever a user-facing function is **added, changed or improved** in any of them,
    the **same working step** records it in the Decision Log of that project **and** adds a row to the incoming table of **each
    of the two other projects**:
    - into Android: `D:\Notio-Android\docs\AGENT_EN.md` §2.2 "Features to take over from the desktop apps";
    - into Windows: `D:\Notio-Windows\AGENT.md` §5 (from Android) or §5.1 (from Linux);
    - into Linux: `D:\Notio-Linux\AGENT.md` §5 (from Android) or §5.1 (from Windows).
    A row holds the source project and decision ID, one line of what it is, the source version, and `PLANNED`; it stays `PLANNED`
    until the receiving project ships it (`DONE <version>`), adapts it (`ADAPTED: <how>`) or the user rules it platform-only
    (`NOT CARRIED OVER: <why>`). Bug fixes that only restore decided behaviour and internal refactors are not rows; platform
    mechanics (permissions, installers, tray) are translated, not copied. At the start of a working step the agent reads this
    project's incoming tables for `PLANNED` rows that touch the task, and at the end checks that every function it added reached
    the other two. Rule 13 above is the Android -> desktop part of it. The old folders `Notio-Windows/` and `Notio-Linux/` inside this repository were removed by the user on 2026-09-24; the live projects are the two folders named above. (D232)

16. **Plan first, docs current, commit after each significant piece (user, 2026-09-27: "first mark it in the plan, ask if there are
    questions, keep the documentation current as usual, commit after a significant piece of work; and the plans pulled into Notio
    Windows and Linux").** A new batch of requests is first written down, before any code: a Decision Log entry per item (open
    questions listed in it), a checklist in §21.2, the plan in `docs/TODO_IDEAS_RU.md` §0, and rows in the Windows / Linux §5 tables
    (rules 13, 15). Then the user chooses what is built first. Ambiguities are asked one at a time (rules 3, 4) when that item
    starts, not all up front. Every finished item updates in the same step: this file (§21, §22, state line), `FUNCTIONALITY_RU.md`,
    `TODO_IDEAS_RU.md`, the in-app help texts in all languages and the README when the product description changes. After each
    **significant** finished piece (a Decision Log item built, tested and documented, or a release build) the agent **commits
    without waiting to be asked** (§1.1.1): code and docs together, a message that says why, no AI attribution. Small in-between
    edits are not committed on their own. The desktop repositories are committed the same way once they have their first commit
    made by the user. (D233)

17. **Every new version gets both APKs (user, 2026-09-27: "did you build debug AND release apk? the rule is mandatory: when a new
    version is released (versioning) we build both for me for testing").** Whenever `versionName` / `versionCode` go up, the same
    working step builds **both** `dist/Notio-by-vibic777-<version>-debug.apk` and `dist/Notio-by-vibic777-<version>-release.apk`:
    `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug :app:assembleRelease` (JDK 21), then — while there is no
    release key (D133) — `zipalign -p 4` and `apksigner sign` with the debug keystore, `apksigner verify --print-certs` showing the
    same certificate as the previous build, so it installs over earlier ones. Both sizes and SHA-256 go into the state line and §22.
    A version is not reported as ready while one of the two is missing. (D240)

18. **A code check always covers reliability, economy and performance (user, 2026-09-27: "go through all the code after the fix,
    check the quality: stability, reliability; we already had the rule — saving the battery, these are mobile devices; performance,
    so that we do not eat resources; reliability, stable code that does not crash. Write into the rules: when a check of the whole
    code is asked, take these points into account").** Whenever the user asks to check / go through the code (all of it or after a
    fix), the pass looks at, and reports on, each of these, besides correctness:
    - **stability / reliability:** no crash paths (unchecked `!!`, index / parse errors on user data, exceptions escaping coroutines,
      receivers, services and callbacks), no endless or unbounded loops (a `while (true)` must have a proven exit), no hang of the main
      thread (no blocking I/O, `runBlocking` or heavy work there), state that survives recomposition, rotation and process death;
    - **battery / economy:** nothing polls, wakes or runs in the background without need (alarms exact only where the user asked for a
      time, no periodic jobs without reason, no wake locks, sensors, location or network kept on), foreground services stopped as soon
      as their work is done, listeners and callbacks unregistered;
    - **performance / resources:** no heavy work in composition (parse / sort / decode once, `remember` with the right keys), lists
      lazy where they can be long, bitmaps decoded at the size shown and released, streams and cursors closed, no unbounded caches or
      logs.
    Findings are fixed or written into `TODO_IDEAS_RU.md` with a reason; the pass and its results go into §22. The earlier passes are
    D221 and D231. (D243)

19. **The build stack is written down for moving the project (user, 2026-09-27: "describe the whole build stack and the libraries in a
    recommendations file, so that if the local repository moves from one computer to another the model can quickly set everything up
    again and go on working; this is a rule, also for Notio Windows and Linux").** `docs/DEV_SETUP.md` here, `DEV_SETUP.md` in
    `D:\Notio-Windows` and `D:\Notio-Linux`: what to install (JDK, SDK, tools, versions, paths), the libraries and plugins, the commands
    to build / test / release, what does not travel through git (keys, `local.properties`). Updated in the same working step as any
    change of the stack; read first on a new computer. (D258)

20. **Clean up after every build step (user, 2026-09-27: "after a build step can you clean up after yourself on drive C, as a rule,
    you are filling it; the Temp folder is full too; so that garbage is not left in such amounts; for the main project and Notio
    Windows and Linux").** Nothing a build or a test run makes may stay in the system's Temp or elsewhere on drive C: the unit tests
    get `java.io.tmpdir` = `app/build/test-tmp` (emptied before, removed after the run, in `app/build.gradle.kts`); the intermediate
    file of the release signing is `app/build/tmp/al.apk`, removed after signing; other scratch goes into the project's `build/`.
    When a step made something outside the project anyway, it is removed in the same step, only what that step made (never other
    programs' files). The desktop projects do the same (their test tasks and packaging). (D259)
    **Also inside the project (user, 2026-09-27: "clean up the garbage also after a build in the local repository of the project, Temp /
    tmp files and folders if there are any: if it piles up the project grows; files and folders that are not needed must be removed").**
    After every build step run `tools/clean_after_build.sh`: it stops the Gradle daemons, removes the temp folders the build makes in
    the project (`build/sqlite-tmp`, `app/build/test-tmp`, `app/build/tmp/test*`, `al.apk`, `*.idsig`) and our leftovers in the system
    Temp, names any `temp` / `tmp` folder outside `build/` for a look, and prints the sizes. Build outputs (compiled classes,
    intermediates, APKs) are not garbage and stay (removing them only slows the next build); `dist/` keeps the APKs of every version
    until the owner says which to drop. The desktop projects get the same script for their build.

### 1.1.1 Git rules

- The repository is the project root. Local identity:
  `user.name = vibic`, `user.email = (the author's private address, not published)` (set in the **local** repo
  config only; never touch global git config).
- Commit when the user asks for it **and, since 2026-09-27 (rule 16, D233), on its own after each significant
  finished piece of work** (a Decision Log item built, tested and documented; a release build). Commit
  messages describe the *why*; keep `AGENT_EN.md` changes in the same commit as
  the work they record. Nothing is pushed unless the user asks.
- Do not commit secrets, `local.properties`, keystores, or build output.
- **The only author and contributor is the user (user, 2026-09-24: "make only me the contributor, no traces of AI agents").**
  No `Co-Authored-By` or any other AI attribution line in commit messages, tags or release notes; the in-app credit is
  "Developer: vibic". The earlier trailers were removed from the whole history on 2026-09-24 (a backup bundle of the old
  history is kept outside the repository, `../Notio-Android-backup-2026-09-24.bundle`).
- Current state of the repository: see §21.0.

### 1.2 Implementation rules

- Kotlin as the primary language.
- Jetpack Compose as the primary UI approach.
- MVVM + unidirectional data flow.
- Business logic must not live in Composable functions.
- Repositories and `Flow` for data.
- Any operation touching multiple tables must run in a transaction.
- Do not use `Float` / `Double` for money. Use `Long` in minor units
  (cents / kopecks).
- All dates and times are stored in UTC as epoch millis.
- Entity IDs are UUIDs.
- Database migrations are mandatory.
- Destructive migrations in production mode are forbidden.

### 1.3 Dangerous action rule

Any deletion must show a confirmation dialog explaining:

- what is being deleted;
- which data will be affected;
- whether data will be moved;
- whether the action can be undone;
- whether the deletion is irreversible.

Actions requiring confirmation:

- deleting a folder;
- deleting a note;
- deleting a budget item;
- deleting a reminder;
- deleting a notification sound;
- resetting security settings;
- deleting the featured note of a folder if it causes data loss;
- full app reset.

---

## 2. Short product description

**NoteBudget** — an Android note-taking app with extended capabilities:

- note types: text (Markdown), drawing (raster), list, budget, audio; the set of kinds is
  extensible (D82);
- mandatory folder organization of notes;
- folders with name, color, style, and optional icon;
- a featured note per folder whose totals can be shown on the folder card;
- reminders and notifications, including repeating ones;
- app lock (password, 3×3 pattern, login/password, biometrics);
- themes, localization RU / EN / FR / DE;
- budget with a recursive node tree and cascading recalculation;
- export/import (full backup and individual notes);
- tags, search, trash.

### 2.1 Sibling platforms (user, 2026-09-22; separate projects since 2026-09-24 — see rules 13 and 15)

Android (this repository) is the flagship, the only one with shipped releases. Two more platform projects exist around the
same product, each a **separate project outside this repository** (since 2026-09-24): `D:\Notio-Windows` — **started**
(Gradle KMP: `shared` + `desktopApp`, its D10) — and `D:\Notio-Linux` — planning only so far. Features move between all
three by rule 15. This section is the map between them; rule 13
(§1.1) is the process that keeps it from going stale. **An iOS project was planned too and was dropped by the user on
2026-09-22 (D205); it is not part of the plan any more.**

- **`D:\Notio-Windows`** and **`D:\Notio-Linux`** — desktop apps, each with two roles in one binary
  (see below): the same note-taking/budget product as a desktop client, **and** an optional
  **sync host** role the other devices connect to. Not a phone client re-skinned: a desktop app first,
  sync second.

**Tech stack (user, 2026-09-22).**

- **Android** — Kotlin + Jetpack Compose, as already shipped; unchanged by this section.
- **`D:\Notio-Windows`** and **`D:\Notio-Linux`** — **Kotlin Multiplatform + Compose Multiplatform
  Desktop**, one shared codebase for both (not two separate native implementations). Reasoning: a
  solo/AI-assisted developer benefits far more from reusing the domain layer's language, idioms and
  (potentially, via Room 2.7+'s KMP support) the persistence code that already exist on Android than
  from a "more native" toolkit (WinUI 3, Avalonia, GTK4, Qt) that would mean redesigning and
  re-maintaining the same business logic 2-3 times over. Packaged via `jpackage`/`jlink` into ordinary
  installers (`.msi`/`.exe`, `.deb`/`.rpm`/AppImage) with a bundled minimal runtime — no separate Java
  install required. Performance is adequate for this workload (a forms/lists/budget-tree/drawing-canvas
  app, not a game or realtime app): Compose Desktop renders through Skia (GPU-accelerated, same engine
  as Android/Chrome), typical cold start is sub-2s and baseline memory ~150-250 MB, both irrelevant next
  to the actual bottleneck (network/disk I/O, already proven fast on much weaker Android hardware). A
  GraalVM Native Image build is a later escape hatch for near-instant startup if it's ever wanted,
  without a language change.

**Why a desktop app needs a "sync host" role at all.** The user's own framing: a Windows or Linux
machine is usually the thing that is *on* and *reachable on the home network* more often than any
one phone, so it is a natural, convenient rendezvous point — but (see Topology below) it is not the
*only* device that can offer this role. There is deliberately no cloud service anywhere in this design
(matches D16/D45/D75, "no cloud sync"); nothing ever leaves the user's own network to a third party.

**Topology — roles are per-device, not per-platform (user, 2026-09-22, revising the first draft of
this section).** Every Notio app, on every platform including Android, can offer **both**
roles; which one(s) are turned on is the person's own choice on that device, not something the
platform forces:

- **Host role** — the device shows a pairing code and accepts an incoming connection.
- **Connect role** — the device enters another device's pairing code and initiates a connection to it.
- A desktop (Windows/Linux) is simply the device people will *usually* choose to leave in the host
  role, because it is more often on and on the home network — not a hard platform restriction. Two
  phones on the same local network **can** pair and sync directly with each other, one in each role,
  with no desktop involved.
- **Many pairs at once, by design** (the user's own example): two desktops and two phones, all pairing
  and syncing with each other in whatever role combination the person picked on each — every device
  keeps its own set of pairings and syncs with each independently; there is no single "the server," no
  arbitrary cap on how many pairings a device holds.
- **Reachability caveat, stated honestly:** this depends on both devices being reachable to each other
  at sync time — straightforward on the same local network (home Wi-Fi), much harder across separate
  networks (e.g. two phones each on mobile data away from home), since that would need NAT
  traversal or an internet-facing relay, which starts to look like the cloud service this design
  deliberately avoids. **Out of scope for a first version:** two devices on separate networks should go
  through a shared desktop host each of them can reach on its own network at different times, not sync
  directly with each other over the open internet.

**Sync is on-demand, not a background service (user, 2026-09-22 — this replaces any earlier
implication of an always-listening daemon).** Nothing runs continuously and nothing syncs on its own:
both sides must have the app open and the person must explicitly trigger it each time — the host side
turns its host role on (starts listening) and presses **Synchronize**; the connecting side, separately,
also presses **Synchronize**. This removes any need to fight Android's background-execution limits
(Doze): the app never needs to run or listen while it is not in the
foreground. What *is* automatic, per the earlier framing that first used the word "auto": once a sync is
triggered, the **merge/add/replace-by-recency** policy (below) resolves conflicts by its own rule,
without the person adjudicating each item by hand — "automatic" describes the conflict policy, not the
timing of when a sync happens. **Background execution in this app stays scoped to what it is already
for** — reminders/alarms (D10, D122, D150 on Android; their own platform mechanisms on the desktop),
which genuinely need to fire while the app is closed and are unaffected by any of this. Sync adds no
new background-execution requirement of its own.

**Pairing (first connection) persists past that one session.** A short-lived, human-readable **pairing
code**, shown by the device in the host role and typed once into the device in the connect role: the two
sides derive a shared secret from it (first planned as a PAKE such as SPAKE2; shipped in D226 as ECDH P-256 with HMAC proofs keyed by the
PBKDF2-stretched code — either way the code itself is never
sent over the network and a network eavesdropper who doesn't also see the screen learns nothing) and
each side stores the resulting long-term credential for that pairing. Only the code entry is one-time;
every *sync* after that still needs the manual trigger above on both sides, but neither side re-enters
the code — the stored credential is what a later connection authenticates with. Un-pairing (either side)
revokes that one credential without touching any other pairing.

**Connection security (the user's own three points, 2026-09-22).**

1. **Encrypted channel** — TLS (or an equivalent authenticated-encryption transport; shipped in D226 as AES-256-GCM frames) for every
   sync connection, always, not optional.
2. **Authenticate the connection before exchanging any data** — the pairing credential (above)
   proves both sides before a single note crosses the wire, so an unpaired device on the same network
   gets refused at the handshake, not after it has already received something.
3. **Pairing by code**, detailed above — this *is* point 3 from the user's message; it doubles as
   the authentication mechanism for point 2 (the long-term credential it produces is what every later
   connection authenticates with).

**Test connection / diagnostics (user, 2026-09-22).** Mirrors two patterns this app already has for
exactly this kind of "does the network thing actually work" uncertainty: the SMTP recovery mail's
**"send a test message"** (D129) and the reminder alarm's **"test the alarm in 10 seconds"** (D152). A
**"Test connection"** action attempts a real handshake against a paired device (or the pairing-in-progress
target) without transferring any notes, and reports a specific outcome (reached / refused / wrong code /
TLS failed / timed out — not just "failed"). Its attempts feed a local-only **diagnostics log** — shipped in
D226 as the per-connection sync log; a general app log followed in D230 (`core/common/AppLog.kt`, Settings -> About ->
App log). Same privacy rule as everywhere else
in this app (§13, INV-11): **never logs secrets or credentials**, never leaves the device on its own, and
is viewable/exportable on demand the same way the budget log already is (D166: a screen, TXT/PDF export)
rather than sent anywhere automatically.

**What syncs, and how conflicts resolve — needs its own Decision Log entries once a sibling
project's real design phase starts** (this section fixes the shape, not the final rules); the
starting point, from the user's own framing:

- **Direction** is configurable per pairing: push only, pull only, or two-way.
- **Mode** is configurable per pairing: **merge** (like the existing JSON backup's merge mode,
  §6.13/D63 — add what's missing, keep both on a real conflict), **add as new copies** (never
  overwrite, always duplicate — the existing backup's "add" mode, same reference), or **replace by
  recency** (last-writer-wins per record, using `updated_at`, already stored in UTC epoch millis on
  every record per §7.1 — the two clocks being compared do need to agree, which the sync protocol
  itself must guarantee, e.g. by exchanging timestamps at connect time rather than trusting each
  device's wall clock blindly).
- Whichever mode: sync operates at the same granularity the existing backup/restore code already
  understands (folder, note, budget node, reminder, tag, comment, trash entry — see §7), so the sync
  layer is a network transport for substantially the same merge logic §6.13 already has, not a new
  data model.

**Decided for Android in D226 (2026-09-24)**, and binding for the desktop projects so that all devices speak one protocol: local
network only, discovery by mDNS / DNS-SD (`_notio-sync._tcp`, TXT `id`, `n`, `pair`) with a typed address as fallback, TCP port
47821 (else any free port), protocol v1 = plain hellos + ECDH P-256 + HKDF-SHA256 + HMAC proofs + AES-256-GCM frames, pairing by a
12-character code stretched with PBKDF2, the data = the backup stream of chosen folders, imported with the backup's own modes.
No relay, no port forwarding, no internet (rule 14: cloud on pause). Details: D226.


### 2.2 Features to take over from the desktop apps (rule 15)

Functions born in `Notio-Windows` or `Notio-Linux` that the Android app must get as well. Filled by the desktop projects' agents in the same
working step as the desktop decision; Android works them off like its own requirements (Decision Log entry first).

| From | What it is | Source version | Android |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Windows D36 | **A photo note takes a picture file too**: next to "Take photo" a "Picture…" button adds an existing image (JPEG as it is, any other picture put on white and saved as JPEG) as a new item of the note | 0.9.0 | PLANNED |
| Windows D12 | **Demo data**: File -> Load demo data imports a demo backup (three folders, one note of every kind the desktop shows, a budget with a tree and a log, tags, a reminder, a trashed note) through the ordinary import (MERGE), for trying the app and for testing Since Windows 0.9.37 (D97) also **Delete demo data**: only the demo notes and folders that were not changed since the load go, for good. | 0.1.0 | NOT CARRIED OVER: a test aid of the Windows app only (user, 2026-09-24: "only a test tool for Windows, not needed in Android") |

## 3. Glossary

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| **Folder** | Container for notes. Every note belongs to a folder. |
| **Note** | A user entity of one of the types: TEXT, DRAWING, LIST, BUDGET. |
| **Featured Note** | A note inside a folder chosen as the main one; its totals may be shown on the folder card. |
| **Budget Note** | A note of type BUDGET containing a recursive node tree. |
| **Budget Node** | A node in the budget tree: `LEAF` (manual values) or `GROUP` (computed values). |
| **Leaf** | A budget node with manually entered sum and goal. |
| **Group** | A budget node whose values are computed from children. |
| **Reminder** | A scheduled event attached to a note. |
| **App Lock** | The mechanism that locks app entry. |
| **Backup** | A file containing exported data. |
| **Trash** | Storage for soft-deleted notes and folders. |

Fixed 1080x1440 sheet; tools pencil / brush / eraser, colour, width, undo (last 50 strokes, older ones are baked into the image) | ACCEPTED (agent technical decision within D39) |
| D84 | G09 resolved: full backup = ZIP with `backup.json` + `attachments/<noteId>/<file>`, name `notebudget-backup-YYYY-MM-DD.json.zip`. **Compatibility rule (user: mandatory):** every field has a default, readers ignore unknown fields, enums travel as strings, unknown note kinds are kept, `formatVersion` is bumped only for changes old readers cannot read and then `minReaderVersion` is raised; a reader refuses (never half-imports) a file whose `minReaderVersion` exceeds what it supports. A golden v1 file is a test that must keep passing | ACCEPTED (user requirement, agent design) |
| D85 | Import modes (D63): MERGE keeps everything, same id -> newer `updatedAt` wins, a folder with the same name (case-insensitive) as an existing one is folded into it (D52); NEW_IDS adds everything as new copies; REPLACE wipes then loads (refused if the backup has no active folder, INV-2). Always: unsafe ids rejected (path traversal), notes with a missing folder go to a fallback folder (INV-1), broken featured links cleared (INV-3), all budget caches recomputed (INV-13), alarms re-armed. All-or-nothing: one DB transaction. Single-note files: TEXT `.md`, DRAWING `.png`/`.jpg`, AUDIO `.m4a`, LIST/BUDGET JSON (structure) or text snapshot (comes back as TEXT, D24); imported notes always get a new id and the chosen folder (INV-12) | ACCEPTED (agent technical decision within D22-D24, D62, D63) |

| D87 | Requested by the user 2026-09-20: budget order is per note. `BudgetLayout` in `note.extras`: overall total at the bottom, group totals below their items, newest items first. Defaults keep the old look | ACCEPTED (user request) |
| D88 | Requested by the user 2026-09-20: audio note = one `.m4a` (AAC, mono, 44.1 kHz, 64 kbit/s) per note in `filesDir/audio`, recorded with `MediaRecorder`, played with `MediaPlayer`. `RECORD_AUDIO` is asked on the first tap on Record. Recording is finished and kept when the app goes to the background (no foreground service). Recording again replaces the clip only if the new recording is valid | ACCEPTED (user request) |
| D89 | App settings: language (system / en / ru / fr / de, applied in `MainActivity.attachBaseContext` + `recreate()`), theme (system / light / dark), reminder notification privacy, help + glossary (localized), backup entry points. Language and theme travel in backups, applied only on REPLACE | ACCEPTED (agent technical decision within D15, D60, §6.11) |
| D90 | **Confirmed by the user 2026-09-20:** a drawing note is a **hybrid of two layers**, raster + vector (the session that started it was closed by accident, the user asked to finish it). RASTER layer (D83, `drawings/<id>.png`): pencil, brush, eraser, bucket fill (`FloodFill`, tolerance 48 per channel, 4 neighbours), undo by replaying `RasterOp`s (`RasterHistory`, last 50, older ones baked in). VECTOR layer on top (`drawings-vector/<id>.json`, `VectorDocument`): line, arrow, rectangle, ellipse, triangle, free-hand path (Ramer-Douglas-Peucker simplified); select / move / drag end handles, fill, stroke colour and width, duplicate, delete, z-order, undo (50 steps). Each layer can be hidden. Shapes are stored with a `type` string so a shape kind from a newer app is kept and re-saved untouched. Export: PNG / JPEG flatten both layers (D42); a third format "Layers" (JSON) keeps the shapes editable. Backup: `drawing.png` + `vector.json` attachments; an old backup without `vector.json` restores as raster-only; a damaged `vector.json` is skipped, never blocks the import (D84) | ACCEPTED (user) |
| D91 | TEXT editor (within D38): the note stays plain Markdown in `contentText`, so old notes open unchanged. | D92 | **Requested by the user 2026-09-20 (list notes), refined the same day.** Built on 2026-09-20, see D96. (1) Besides its text, every list item has an optional **comment** (D70), a **quantity** and a **unit**. (2) **Units are an app setting** (list of units in Settings). Built-in units that can never be deleted: **pieces, kilograms, litres, metres and the currencies rouble, US dollar, pound sterling, euro**. The user can add custom units and delete custom ones only (D69); what happens to items that still use a deleted custom unit is decided when it is built (default: refuse while in use). Built-in units have stable ids (`pcs`, `kg`, `l`, `m`, `rub`, `usd`, `gbp`, `eur`) and localized names; units travel in backups. (3) A list can contain **unit totals** (the user's "summator"; the UI name chosen by the agent is **"Total by unit"**): a special row with a **name**, a **comment** and a chosen **unit**; its result is the **sum of the quantities of all items of that list that use that unit**. Any number of unit totals with different units can be in one list. Old lists and backups must keep loading (D84, D86) | ACCEPTED (user request), IMPLEMENTED (D96) |
| D93 | **Trash (Phase 10), agent technical decision within D29, D65, D81.** A Trash screen (bin icon in the main top bar) lists trashed folders and notes, newest first; notes trashed together with a folder are counted in the folder row, a note trashed earlier stays a row of its own. **Restore:** a note goes back to its folder, or, when that folder is itself in the trash, the user picks an active folder (`NoteUseCases.restoreInto`); a folder returns with the notes trashed with it and needs its name to be free (D52). **Delete forever** (per item) and **Empty trash** always ask for confirmation (INV-6, §1.3); they are irreversible and remove the note, its budget nodes and reminders (FK cascade), its alarms and its files (`NoteTransferHandler.deleteAttachments`, wired through `NoteDataCleaner`). A trashed folder never holds active notes: purging refuses otherwise (`FOLDER_NOT_EMPTY`), and the database transaction rolls back. **Retention** (D65): 7 / 14 / **30 (default)** / 60 / 90 days or never, setting `trashRetentionDays` (DataStore; travels in backups, applied on REPLACE; absent in older backups). Expired items are removed at every app start (`AppContainer.initialize`, no background job), each by its own `deletedAt`; a folder expires with the notes trashed with it. No schema change (`deleted_at` exists since D81) | ACCEPTED (agent technical decision) |

Display: the quantity next to the name ("Bread ×3", shown when above 1) and a line with the per-piece values. A GROUP has no quantity (always 1, INV-4). Storage: `budget_node.quantity INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, **Room schema v3** with migration 2→3 (existing items count once) and a `MigrationTest`; backup and single-note files carry `quantity` (default 1: files without it load as 1, a nonsense value falls back to 1; an older app that ignores the field counts each item once when it recomputes). | D95 | **Drawing rules after the user's phone test (2026-09-20), agent technical decision.** (1) Select tool: a shape is picked by touching its outline or, when filled, inside; otherwise by touching inside a closed shape (the smallest one under the finger); the slack is 14 dp of screen, not sheet pixels. (2) Finger jitter under half the slack is a tap, not a drag (no move, no undo step). (3) A newly drawn shape is not left selected; the selection frame shows only with the select tool. (4) The vector fill tool paints the topmost closed shape the touch is inside of, outline-only shapes included; choosing it while the fill is "none" picks a default colour (amber), an explicit "no fill" afterwards clears the fill. (5) The toolbar recomposes on discrete events only (touch down/up, tool, undo), not on every finger move. (6) The raster eraser shows a ring of its real size at the last touch (the sheet centre before the first touch) and a small eraser next to it | ACCEPTED (agent technical decision) |
| D96 | **List notes, how D92 was built (agent technical decisions).** (1) **Storage, no schema change:** the structure of a list (rows with text, done, comment, quantity, unit; unit-total rows) is JSON in `note.extras` (`ListDocument`), and `contentText` always holds a readable text copy (`- [ ] Milk — 2 L (whole)`, `= Kilos: 5 kg`) for search, previews, text export and older apps. `textHash` (hash of that copy) is stored in the structure: if `contentText` no longer matches (an older app edited the list, or the text changed elsewhere) the text wins and rows are rebuilt from it, unit names kept. Lists made before D92 have no structure and read as plain items. Every field has a default, unknown fields are ignored, unknown row kinds are kept (D84). (2) **Quantity** is `Long` thousandths (up to 3 decimals, negative allowed, blank = none); sums are exact. (3) **Units** are stored by id: built-in `pcs kg l m rub usd gbp eur` (localized symbol + name), custom ones are `{id (UUID), name (1..24 chars)}` kept in the settings (DataStore) and carried in backups (`SettingsRecord.customUnits`, applied on REPLACE). A list also carries the names of the custom units it uses, so it reads right on another phone; opening such a list adds those units to this phone (`UnitUseCases.adopt`, skipped when the id is known or the name is taken). (4) **Unit totals** ("Total by unit") sum the quantities of ALL items of the list with the same unit, checked or not; a total never counts another total; no unit chosen = 0. (5) **Custom unit rules:** names unique ignoring case and not equal to a built-in name; a custom unit that any list uses (item or total, trashed lists included) cannot be deleted; built-in units cannot be deleted; deleting asks for confirmation. (6) The note editor passes `extras` and one combined change callback to the editor of a kind (`NoteTypeDefinition.Editor(..., extras, onEdit)`), and `NoteUseCases.save(withExtras)` writes `extras` only when the editor changed them, so renaming or editing text never overwrites structure saved elsewhere | ACCEPTED (agent technical decision) |

(2) **Data versions, each guarded by a test:** database schema `AppDatabase.VERSION` (now 3; a test checks the migrations are an unbroken chain 1..VERSION and that every version has its exported schema file), backup and note file format `TransferFormat.FORMAT_VERSION` (1, with `minReaderVersion`, D84), and a `v` / `formatVersion` field inside the list structure and the vector layer. (3) **Settings → About** shows: "NoteBudget, version X (build N)", "Developer: vibic777 + AI" (no specific model is named anywhere in the app), and "Backup format 1 · database version 3". (4) **Languages:** the app, the help and the glossary exist in EN, RU, FR, DE; a test fails the build if a string, a plural, or a help text is missing or left in English in any language, or a format placeholder differs | ACCEPTED (user request) |

| D124 | **App lock (D121, §6.1, Phase 5).** Settings → App lock. Methods: **password** (4-64 characters), **3×3 pattern** (4 to 9 different dots), **login + password** (one local account; no roles, G03). **Fingerprint / face** through the system prompt as an *addition* (the password or pattern always stays as the fallback) and only if the phone has it. **Timeout:** right away, 30 s, 1 min (default), 5 min, 15 min, or only at a fresh start; a screen rotation is not "leaving". **Recovery (G02):** an optional security question (five ready ones); a right answer lets the person set a new *password*; without a question a forgotten secret cannot be recovered (the screen says so; reinstalling deletes the data, a backup file can be imported). **Brute force:** from the fifth wrong try in a row the app makes the person wait 30 s, doubling each time up to 15 min, and even the right secret waits. Only **salted PBKDF2 hashes** (`CredentialHasher`, 210 000 iterations) are stored, in a private preferences file of their own (`allowBackup=false`), **never in a backup** (INV-11); a file that cannot be read means "no lock". While the lock is on the window is `FLAG_SECURE` (no screenshots, blank recent-apps picture). The lock screen replaces the whole app (nothing is composed behind it); changing the secret or turning the lock off needs the current secret. Deviation from §7.7: hashes are in private preferences, not in Keystore-encrypted storage. | ACCEPTED (part of D121). **STATUS 0.5.0: DONE in code and tests, not run on a device.** `AppLockUseCases` (17 tests: setup rules, verify, throttle, disable, change, recover, options, `LockPolicy`, `PatternGrid`, stored-form compatibility), `AppLockGate`, `SharedPreferencesLockStore`, `BiometricAuth` (framework `android.hardware.biometrics`, no new dependency), `LockScreen`, `PatternPad`, `SecretForm`, `LockSettingsScreen`; one UI flow (the lock covers the app until unlocked). **Unverified on a device: the pattern pad gestures, the biometric prompt, FLAG_SECURE, the timeout after really leaving the app. Not built:** roles and several accounts, e-mail recovery, Keystore encryption of the hashes, a lock for single notes. |
| D125 | **Credit and icon (user, 2026-09-20).** (1) The developer credit becomes **"vibic777 + the AIs"** in the plural and playful form the user chose in Russian ("vibic777 + ИИшки"); translated as **EN "Developer: vibic777 + the AIs", RU "Разработчик: vibic777 + ИИшки", FR "Développeur : vibic777 + les IA", DE "Entwickler: vibic777 + die KIs"**; a specific AI model is still never named (the test keeps EN exact and "vibic777" in every language). (2) **Launcher icon:** the background is **blue** (`#1E5FD8`, was green), the note sheet stays white, its lines are blue, and **red text (`#E53935`) is drawn over the sheet: "vibic" at the upper left and "777" at the lower right** (vector strokes, no font, inside the adaptive-icon safe zone so round and squircle masks keep it) | ACCEPTED (user request). **STATUS 0.5.1: DONE.** `drawable/ic_launcher_foreground.xml`, `values/colors.xml`, `about_developer` x4. Checked only by a drawn preview and by the build; how the icon looks on the launcher is for the user to judge (the letters are small at launcher size). |
| D126 | **A reminder notification opens its note (user question, 2026-09-20).** Tapping the notification goes straight to the note of the reminder (before, it only opened the app). The tap starts or brings forward the activity with `OpenNoteRequest.ACTION_OPEN_NOTE` and the note id (a data URI `notebudget://note/<id>` keeps the pending intent unique per note); `MainActivity` hands it to `OpenNoteRequest` (in `onCreate` and `onNewIntent`), the navigation opens `NoteEditor(id)` and clears the request so a turn of the screen does not open it twice. **With the app lock on the request waits: the lock screen comes first, the note opens after the unlock.** A note that no longer exists (deleted, in the trash) is skipped and the request forgotten. The privacy setting (generic / title only / full text) still decides what the notification SHOWS; the tap always opens the note (behind the lock). | ACCEPTED (answer to a question, then implemented). **STATUS 0.5.2: DONE, not run on a device** (only the intent handling and the navigation are tested, not a real notification tap). Tests: 3 UI tests (opens the note, a gone note is forgotten, a foreign intent is ignored). |
| D127 | **Blueprint toolbar stuck (user report on 0.5.1, 2026-09-20).** In a blueprint the tool chips did not follow the taps: after choosing the ellipse the chip still showed "Select", the tool seemed to lag one choice behind, later nothing could be chosen (not even Fill) and then Fill suddenly "worked" without being shown; shapes could not be selected. The same symptom had been reported on 0.2.0 and "fixed" by passing a revision number down; that fix was never confirmed on a phone and was only reasoned about. | ACCEPTED (user report). **STATUS 0.5.2: FIXED, reproduced first, NOT confirmed on a phone.** A Robolectric test now taps the chips one after another and reads their `selected` state: **it failed before the change** (the toolbar was composed again with the right tool, yet the chips did not show it) and passes now. Cause: the toolbar and the layers panel read plain fields of `VectorEditor`; whatever reached them depended on a parent handing down a changing number. Fix: everything they show is now **Compose state of the view model** (`vectorTool`, `selectedShape`, stroke and fill colour and width, `layersState`, active layer, can-draw), copied from the editor after every discrete change (not on finger moves), and the chips, colour rows, width and layers read those states directly. Test: `theBlueprintToolChipsFollowEveryTap`. |
| D128 | **More languages (user, 2026-09-20).** Add **Chinese, Belarusian, Japanese, Korean, Spanish and Italian** to the four we have (English, Russian, French, German). | ACCEPTED (user request). **STATUS: DONE in 0.7.0 (D132), JVM-tested, translations NOT yet read by native speakers.** The app's translations are written by the agent (no native reviewer), so they need a read-through by native speakers before a public release; the language switch in Settings, `locales_config.xml`, the language list of the settings backup and the localization tests must all learn the new tags (`zh`, `be`, `ja`, `ko`, `es`, `it`). Long help texts are the bulk of the work. |
| D129 | **Lock: any timeout and three ways to recover (user, 2026-09-20), with the reminder sounds.** (1) The lock timeout keeps its presets and gains **Other...**: any minutes + seconds (1 s to 23 h 59 min 59 s; `LockTimeouts`). (2) Recovery is now **three methods**, each optional and each set up in Settings -> App lock after typing the current secret: the **security question** (as before); **a code by e-mail through the user's own SMTP server** (host, port, encryption none / STARTTLS / SSL-TLS, login, password, from, recipient; the app makes a 6-digit code, keeps only its hash, valid 10 min, 5 tries, a new code not earlier than 60 s; the SMTP client is our own, on raw sockets, hostname-verified TLS, AUTH PLAIN/LOGIN); **an authenticator app** (TOTP RFC 6238: SHA-1, 6 digits, 30 s, +-1 step; the secret is shown as a QR code (ZXing core) and as text and must be confirmed with one code before it counts). After a successful recovery the person sets a new secret. (3) **This closes gap G02** and overrides D16 / D75 for this one feature only: the app now asks for the `INTERNET` permission, used **only** to send that one mail to the server the user typed in; there is no server of ours. The e-mail password and the TOTP key are stored encrypted with an Android Keystore AES-GCM key (`SecretBox`), in private preferences, never in a backup. (4) Password form: a mismatch of the two fields is shown under the field at once and the button stays disabled (the user's earlier failure to enable the lock was an unnoticed mismatch). (5) **Reminder sound (user)**: the reminder dialog chooses the melody like an alarm clock: the phone's notification / ringtone / alarm sounds through the system picker, or **the person's own audio file (mp3 and others)** copied to the app's private folder and handed to the system through a `FileProvider`; "Listen" plays it; Reset returns the default. (6) **Defect fixed: notifications played no sound.** The notification channel now gets its sound explicitly (alarm usage), one channel per sound + vibration (`rem2_<sound>_<vib>`), and a second, own playback path exists (D130). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.6.0, JVM-tested (`AppLockUseCasesTest`, `AppLockRecoveryTest`, `SecurityCodesTest` with RFC vectors and a fake SMTP server, `ReminderChannelsTest`), NEVER RUN on a phone.** Only plain SMTP was tested against a fake server: STARTTLS and SSL/TLS, the QR scan, the mail delivery, the file picker and the FileProvider grant are unchecked. Schema unchanged by this decision. |
| D130 | **Reminders like an alarm clock (user, 2026-09-20).** (1) **Extra rings:** "repeat like an alarm clock": X more rings every N minutes (0-50 rings, 1-1440 min), separate from "repeat until confirmed". (2) **Volume and fade-in:** volume 10-100 % (relative to the phone's alarm volume) and a gradual rise from a quiet start (5 %) to the chosen level over Off / 10 / 30 / 60 / 120 s (`RingRamp`). When the volume is below 100 or the rise is on, the sound is played by our own foreground media service `ReminderRingService` (MediaPlayer, alarm usage, loops up to 60 s, a Silence action in the notification); if that service cannot start (the system refuses a foreground service from the alarm), the channel sound plays instead. (3) **Recurrence:** once / **every day** / **chosen weekdays** (Mon..Sun mask; all days = every day) / **every month on day N (1-31)**; a month that has no such day is **skipped** (the 30th skips February, the 31st skips 30-day months; the 29th appears in leap years). The time of day is kept across clock changes (`RecurrenceRules.next`). A recurring reminder moves to its next date when its last ring is done (or when it is confirmed, if confirmation is asked) and never reaches COMPLETED by itself. (4) **Database v5** (`MIGRATION_4_5`, seven `ALTER TABLE reminder ADD COLUMN` with defaults; `MigrationTest` "4 to 5"): `volume_percent` 100, `ramp_seconds` 0, `repeat_count` 0, `repeats_done` 0, `recurrence` NONE, `weekdays_mask` 0, `month_day` 0. The backup `ReminderRecord` has the same fields with defaults; older backups read as before, wild values are clamped; the backup format version stays 1. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.6.0, JVM-tested (`ReminderRepeatTest`, `MigrationTest`), NEVER RUN on a phone** (the real alarm, the foreground service started from an alarm, the volume curve by ear and the sound after a reboot are unchecked). No snooze yet. |
| D131 | **The application name is "Notio by vibic" (user, 2026-09-20; before it was "Notio by vibic").** `app_name` and the About line in four languages, the mail subjects/bodies, the authenticator issuer, the README plans. The APK is named after the app: Gradle `base.archivesName` = `Notio-by-vibic777-<version>`, so the debug build is `Notio-by-vibic777-<version>-debug.apk` (not `app-debug.apk`). The package id `com.notebudget.app` stays (changing it would break installing over the old build). The user wants to head towards a release; the release build, the signing key and the publication stay for the very end (§21.3). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.6.0** (label checked with `aapt`). |
| D132 | **Next iteration = six more languages (user, 2026-09-20; explains D128).** After 0.6.0 is checked on the phone, the next iteration adds **Chinese (`zh`), Belarusian (`be`), Japanese (`ja`), Korean (`ko`), Spanish (`es`), Italian (`it`)**: `values-<tag>/strings.xml`, the plurals, the long help and glossary texts, `locales_config.xml`, the language list of the settings backup and the localization tests. A draft of the Spanish short strings (625 keys, without the newest reminder keys) is kept in `translations/wip/es_short_keys.tsv`. The agent writes the translations; a native speaker should read them before any public release. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.7.0.** The ten languages: en, ru, be, fr, de, es, it, zh (Simplified), ja, ko; `LocalizationTest` checks every string, plural (per-language CLDR categories), placeholder and help text; `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES`, the language list in Settings, `locales_config.xml` know them. The translations were written by the agent (no native review), the Chinese is Simplified only. Sources and the generator are kept in `translations/` for later corrections. |
| D133 | **Signing keys must be kept and never published (user, 2026-09-20).** The debug builds are signed with the debug key `~/.android/debug.keystore` (outside the repository); a future release will use a **release key** made together with the user just before publication. Rules: **no key, no `keystore.properties`, no password goes to GitHub or into any chat** (`.gitignore` has `*.jks`, `*.keystore`, `keystore.properties`; `git ls-files` shows none); the release key is **never to be lost** (a lost key means the app can no longer be updated for its users) and is kept in two places with its passwords and alias apart; the debug key is copied to a flash drive; a build signed with another key does not install over the old one (data via Export/Import); the project builds on another PC with JDK 21 + Android SDK + the copied key. The agent must **remind the user** of `SIGNING_KEYS_RU.md` before the release build and before any publication, and prepare `keystore.properties` and the Gradle signing config without secrets in the repository. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DOCUMENTED (`SIGNING_KEYS_RU.md`), key not yet made.** |
| D134 | **Password-protected backup export (user, 2026-09-20).** Exporting a backup archive can be protected with a password: **with or without one**, chosen in the app. The password is set once in Settings and can be **kept permanently, so that every export uses it**; it is shown as stars with a **show / remind button**. Design notes (to confirm when built): an encrypted file is a wrapper with a magic header and a version, AES-256-GCM with a key from PBKDF2(password, random salt); an unprotected backup stays exactly as before (format 1, golden files untouched) and a new golden file is added for the protected one; import recognises the wrapper and asks for the password, a wrong password gives a clear message and changes nothing, an older app version refuses the file with the usual "not a backup" message. Because the user wants to see the password again, the stored password is kept reversibly in private preferences encrypted with the Android Keystore (`SecretBox`, as the e-mail password of D129), never inside a backup, never logged. Open questions to settle on the way: whether single-note exports (Markdown, PDF, PNG...) also take the password (proposal: only the app's own JSON / ZIP / backup files, plain formats stay readable everywhere); a hint that a forgotten password means an unreadable backup (no recovery). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0, see D149.** |
| D135 | **The new-note dialog on a tablet lost the name of "Audio" (user, 2026-09-20).** On the phone every kind has its name; on the tablet the last kind showed only its radio button: the dialog (title field + six kinds) is taller than the space a tablet in landscape leaves, and the text area of an `AlertDialog` does not scroll by itself, so the last rows were cut off. Fix: the dialog content scrolls (`verticalScroll`). A UI test could not be added: Robolectric never becomes idle with this dialog open (autofocus of the title field), so the check stays in the phone checklist (§21.4 item 20). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: FIXED in 0.7.0, NOT verified on the tablet.** |
| D136 | **Pins by time, own order of notes, pinned folders (user, 2026-09-20).** A pinned note (or folder) stays before the others and **the one pinned last is the first** (`pinnedAt`); "favourite" stays a star that sorts after the pins. Notes get `sortOrder` (the user's own order inside a folder), folders get `isPinned` / `pinnedAt`. Pinning, the star and the bold title are marks, not edits: **they do not change "modified"** (`NoteUseCases.save` compares title, text, extras and colour). **Database v6** (`MIGRATION_5_6`: `note.pinned_at`, `note.sort_order`, `folder.is_pinned`, `folder.pinned_at`; an old pinned note gets `pinned_at = updated_at`), `MigrationTest` "5 to 6", schema `6.json`; the backup records have the same fields with defaults (format stays 1, the golden files are untouched). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0, JVM-tested, not seen on the phone.** |
| D137 | **Sorting (user, 2026-09-20).** `SortKey` now has: MANUAL, NAME, TAGS, COLOR, TYPE, COUNT (folders), CREATED, CREATED_DATE, CREATED_TIME, UPDATED, UPDATED_DATE, UPDATED_TIME; each ascending or descending. "Day only" orders by calendar day and then by name; "time of day only" by the clock time whatever the day. **Colour** orders by hue (reds, greens, blues ..., greys last, no colour last), so equal colours sit together = the grouping the user asked for (no group headers). **Tags** order by the sorted tag names of a note, untagged last. Pinned first, then (not in "My order") favourites, then the key. Answer to "what other criterion do we have": kind (TYPE), tags, number of notes (folders), favourites and pins. An old stored sort value still decodes. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0, JVM-tested (`FormatsAndOrderTest`, `SortingTest`).** The colour tests were updated to the hue order (the old order was by hex text). |

The format is one setting (`DateTimeFormats`: date style x time style, or "as on the phone"), provided to the UI through `LocalDateTimeFormats`, stored as JSON, part of the settings backup (`dateTimeFormats`). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0.** |
| D139 | **Names of exported files (user, 2026-09-20).** `ExportNaming`: any of the note title, the item title (a recording or photo of a multi-note), a date stamp and a time stamp; the moment is the export, the creation or the change; date order (Y-M-D, D-M-Y, M-D-Y) and separator (`-`, `.`, none), time separator (`-`, `.`, none), 12-hour clock with AM/PM, and what joins the parts (`_`, `-`, space, `.`). A colon is never used. Used by note export, audio clips and media items. Kept in the settings and in the settings backup (`exportNaming`). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0, JVM-tested.** The ZIP entries of an audio note keep "<title> - <clip>.m4a" (older tests, D112). |
| D140 | **The trash never empties itself by default (user, 2026-09-20).** `TrashRetention.DEFAULT_DAYS = NEVER`; the choices stay 7 / 14 / 30 / 60 / 90 days / never. The trash screen has a **"Trash settings" button** that opens Settings on the Trash page and scrolls to the option. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0.** |
| D141 | **Drag and drop with a grip (user, 2026-09-20).** Folder cards and note cards carry a four-arrow grip (drawn by hand); dragging it moves the item, the list scrolls by itself near an edge, the item is dropped where it is; a dragged order is saved and **switches the sort to "My order"**. A pinned item cannot cross into the unpinned group and back. Searching hides the grips. The old "move earlier / later" menu items stay for folders. Own code (`DragReorderState`), no library. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0, NOT tried by a finger** (Robolectric cannot drag): the swap logic is the risky part (§21.4a item 3). |
| D142 | **Zoom sits beside View / Edit (user, 2026-09-20).** The −, percent and + buttons moved from the corner over the page into the row of the View / Edit tabs (`ZoomControl`, `LocalZoomControl`), so they never cover the canvas of a drawing on a tablet and show on a phone too; the row is not scaled by the zoom. **The text note now has View first, then Edit** (it had Edit first) through the same tabs row. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0.** |
| D143 | **Folder colours like note colours (user, 2026-09-20).** The folder editor uses the same `ColorRow` as a note: ready-made colours, the ones picked before, and any colour by RGB / code; a colour that is in no list still shows as chosen. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0.** |
| D144 | **A "Show / Hide" button in every password field (user, 2026-09-20).** `PasswordTextField` is used for the lock password (and its repeat), the recovery form, the mail password and the backup password (also when asking for the password of a protected backup). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0.** PIN-like codes (`NumberPassword` one-time codes) are shown in clear as before. |
| D145 | **Settings as pages with a search (user, 2026-09-20).** Settings is a menu of seven pages (Language and formats, Appearance, Notes and export, Security and privacy, Trash, Backup and restore, Help and about); a search field finds an option by its title, hint, a word of its choices or the page name and opens the page scrolled to it and highlighted. The route `Settings(section, entry)` lets other screens (the trash) open a page directly; Back of such a visit returns to where it came from. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0**; `AppFlowTest` was rewritten for the pages. |
| D146 | **Selection mode in multi-notes (user, 2026-09-20).** Audio, photo, video and screen notes: "Select" shows check marks; the bar has the count, Select all, Clear selection, Delete (asks once) and Done; taps toggle. `SelectionBar`, `SelectionState`, `ConfirmDeleteMany`. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0.** |
| D147 | **Photo, Camera video and Screen recording notes (user, 2026-09-20).** Three kinds (`PHOTO`, `VIDEO`, `SCREEN`), each a multi-note like AUDIO: a list of `MediaItem` in `note.extras` (JSON, no schema change), files in `filesDir/media/<noteId>_<itemId>.<jpg|mp4>` with a thumbnail next to them (made when first needed). Every item: name, comment, created / changed, duration, size, place. Photos open full screen in a pager with pinch zoom; videos play in the note or full screen; the app does not lock the orientation and `MainActivity` handles configuration changes itself (a turn does not restart it, so a recording goes on). **Camera:** CameraX; the list of cameras the phone offers (back, front, others); a photo is composed on a bitmap (turned upright by EXIF, overlays drawn, JPEG); a video is recorded by the CameraX recorder. **Sound** of a video / screen recording: none, microphone, the phone's own sound (`AudioPlaybackCapture`, Android 10+, needs the screen-capture permission), or both mixed (16-bit mono 44.1 kHz recorded apart, summed with clipping, written as WAV). A plain microphone goes into the camera video by CameraX itself; anything with the phone's sound is put on afterwards. **Screen recording:** a foreground service (`ScreenRecordService`, types mediaProjection and microphone) with a `MediaRecorder` on a virtual display (H.264, at most 1920 px on the long side, 30 fps); the app steps aside (`moveTaskToBack`); **Stop is in the notification and, with "Display over other apps", a small draggable red button over all windows (marked as sensitive content on Android 15+, which keeps it out of recordings; on older Android it can show in the recording)**; a stop saves at once. The last step (`RecordingFinisher` + `VideoProcessor`): **Media3 Transformer** puts the overlays and the sound on the video in one pass; if that fails the plain video is kept. The screen recorder keeps the size of the screen at the start (a turn during a recording letterboxes). Export: one item as its file, several as a ZIP, named by D139. **Backup: files up to 40 MB each travel in it, bigger videos are left out** (memory); overlay settings and overlay pictures are not part of a backup. New permissions: CAMERA, ACCESS_FINE/COARSE_LOCATION, FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PROJECTION, FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MICROPHONE, SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW (each asked at the moment of use; a refused one takes away only its own feature). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0 as code, JVM-tested for the data side only; NEVER RUN on a device.** Likeliest trouble spots: the Media3 overlay size (a full-frame bitmap is assumed), the mix of a WAV sequence with the video, the foreground-service start on Android 14+, CameraX camera-id filters. |
| D148 | **Overlays and the place (user, 2026-09-20).** One `OverlaySettings` per kind (photo, video, screen), edited in a dialog with a live preview: **time stamp** (date and / or time, own date and time style, colour, size, position, plate), **place** (coordinates `55.75581, 37.61764`, from the system location manager without Google services; permission asked at use), **text** (a signature: text, colour, size, position, plate), **picture** (a logo: any picture, scaled to 1024 px, size as a percent of the frame width, opacity, position, plate). Position is a 3 x 3 grid. "Plate" off = **transparent background**. A time stamp on a video runs with the video (redrawn every second). The place of audio recordings is a switch in Settings → Notes and export (off by default). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0, geometry JVM-tested, drawing not seen.** |
| D149 | **Backup password, how it was built (D134 done).** `BackupCrypto`: file = `NOTIOENC` + version + PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 iterations (200 000) + salt + nonce prefix + 64 KB AES-256-GCM chunks (the "last chunk" flag is in the length and in the authenticated data, so a cut or reordered file is refused). Set in Settings → Backup and restore: switch, password field with Show, Save, a warning; the password is kept encrypted by the Keystore (`SharedPreferencesBackupPasswordStore`), never in a backup or a log. Exports write `.json.zip.enc` when on. Import recognises the wrapper, tries the saved password first and asks otherwise (a wrong one changes nothing). Single-note exports stay plain (readable everywhere). An older app version says "not a backup". | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.0, JVM-tested (round trip, wrong password, cut and changed files, empty file); the Keystore store and the dialogs were not run on a device.** |
| D150 | **Snooze and an alarm-clock screen for reminders (user, 2026-09-21).** (1) **Snooze:** "Snooze N min" on the notification, on the alarm screen and (length in Settings -> Security -> Reminder alarm: 1, 5, 10, 15, 30, 60 min, default 10). It has **its own alarm** (id `snooze-<reminderId>`) and its moment is kept in `reminder.snoozed_until` (**database v7**, `MIGRATION_6_7`, `MigrationTest` "6 to 7"; backup field `snoozedUntil`), so it survives a reboot; a snoozed reminder counts as active for `restoreAll`. A one-time reminder that repeats stops repeating during the snooze and starts again after it; a recurring one keeps its next day; confirming, cancelling, deleting or editing ends a snooze. (2) **Like an alarm clock:** the notification is now `VISIBILITY_PUBLIC` (its text already honours the privacy setting, D60), so its buttons show on the lock screen; with "pop-up" on it carries a **full-screen intent** that opens `ReminderAlarmActivity` over the lock screen and **switches the screen on** (Snooze, Done / Dismiss, Open note). New permission `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT` (sideloaded builds; it overrides the earlier decision not to declare it, gap G08 - Google Play restricts it); Android 14 may need the person to allow it, Settings has a button. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.1, JVM-tested for the snooze logic (`ReminderSnoozeTest`); the alarm screen, the lock-screen buttons and the screen wake-up were NEVER RUN on a device.** The melody still plays once unless the reminder has repeats. |
| D151 | **Folder cards show only the folder (user, 2026-09-21).** The featured-note preview on folder cards is gone (the menu item "Featured note" too; the data stays for old backups), the stamps of folders are no longer on the card (they were on the cards in 0.8.0 and made them big); **auto-sum of folders is dropped** (G04 closed by the user); converting old drawings to blueprints is dropped (the user deletes his two old drawings). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.1.** |
| D152 | **Alarm screen did not appear on Android 14; one clear button; folder cards (user, 2026-09-21).** (1) The user had allowed everything on Android 14 but the alarm did not show over the lock screen. Cause not found without a device; changes that raise the chances: a pop-up reminder is posted with `CATEGORY_ALARM`; **a second path** - with "Display over other apps" allowed the app starts `ReminderAlarmActivity` itself (`ReminderNotifier.startAlarmScreen`); Settings -> Security -> Reminder alarm shows the state of both permissions and has a **"Test the alarm in 10 seconds"** button (lock the phone and watch). (2) The **"Silence" button is gone**: a reminder with confirmation has "Done" (confirms and ends it), any other has "Turn off" (stops the sound and removes the notification); the alarm screen the same, plus Snooze and Open note. (3) Folder cards: the name has its own row (two lines), the count and the small grip / menu buttons a row below, cards 190 dp wide. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.2, NOT verified on a device** - if the alarm still does not show, the test button and the two status lines are the first thing to look at (and the phone's own settings: Do Not Disturb, battery saver, the notification channel set to "pop up"). |

| D154 | **Alarm behaviour after the first phone test (user, 2026-09-21).** (1) **The notification stayed after "Turn off" on the lock screen** (with a melody of its own volume / fade-in the notification is the foreground notification of `ReminderRingService`; `cancel()` from outside does not remove it and the service then only *detached* it): the service now has `ACTION_STOP_REMOVE` (`stopForeground(REMOVE)`), and `ReminderNotifier.dismiss` (Turn off, Done, Repeat) uses it and cancels the channel notification too. The steps are asynchronous (receiver, service, notification manager); the removal now happens inside the service so the order no longer matters. (2) **Only two buttons everywhere:** "Repeat in N min" (the snooze, renamed from "Snooze") and "Turn off" (or "Done" when the reminder asks for confirmation) on the notification and on the alarm screen; "Open note" is gone from the alarm screen (a tap on the notification still opens the note). (3) **A quiet notification stays in the shade while at least one reminder is set** ("Reminders are on: active N, next ..."; low importance, ongoing, secret on the lock screen, tap opens the app; a switch in Settings -> Security -> Reminder alarm, on by default; only if notifications are allowed). It follows the reminders through a collector in `AppContainer.initialize` and after every receiver action (`ReminderSummary`, `ReminderStatusNotification`). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.8.5; the summary is JVM-tested, the notifications were not run on a device.** |

| D156 | **Trash for photos, videos, screen and audio recordings (user, 2026-09-21).** Deleting such an item (one, or several with check marks) takes it out of its note and puts it into the **media trash**: table `media_trash` (**database v8**, `MIGRATION_7_8`, `MigrationTest` "7 to 8"): id = the key of its file, note id, kind, name, the item as JSON, its position, the note title at that moment, when, size. **The file stays where it is.** In the trash screen a card shows kind, size, "from the note X", days left and three buttons: **Restore** (into the same note at the old place; only while the note is active and the file exists), **Export** (streams the file, named by the export settings), **Delete for good**. A note in the trash: restore the note first; a note deleted for good: **restore impossible, export possible**. The trash outlives the note: `deleteAttachments` / `clearAttachments` of the media and audio handlers skip the files of trashed items (`TrashProtection`). Retention (default never) purges these items too; "Empty trash" empties them; the bin badge counts them. **Backup: the list of the media trash travels (`mediaTrash`), its files never do**; a full restore replaces the list, a merge adds what is missing, "new ids" skips it; after an import on the same phone an item can be restored while its file is still there (files of items no longer listed are removed after a full restore). Settings -> Backup says so. Delete dialogs now say the item goes to the trash. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.9.1, JVM-tested (`MediaTrashTest`, migration); the trash screen, the export and the restore dialogs were not run on a device.** |

The card no longer shows a star and the menu no longer has the item; sorting has one tier (pinned, the last pinned first). **Database v11** (`MIGRATION_10_11`): every favourite note becomes pinned (`pinned_at = updated_at`; a note that was both keeps its own pin time) and `is_favorite` is cleared; the column stays (always 0) so old files and code keep working.
