Now `MainActivity` tells the lock gate whenever it opens another window itself (`startActivityForResult`, through which every picker, every "share" and every page of the phone's settings goes), and a return from such a window within `LockPolicy.OWN_WINDOW_MILLIS` (5 minutes) does not ask for the lock. Kept safe: the leave counts only when the app goes out of sight within 3 seconds of opening the window (a window that does not cover the app, as the share sheet, gives no leave to a later leaving); an absence longer than 5 minutes locks by the rule of the settings as before; a start of the process always asks; a return from the alarm screen, from a notification or from another app is as before. The 5 minutes are my choice, the owner may change it. `AppLockUseCasesTest` (the rule and the gate). Windows: no such lock on a file dialog there, nothing for §5. | Done 0.9.102 |
| D347 | **Both ways of a backup are finished: the copy is written to its end, and the file of the database carries the drawings (owner, 2026-10-10: "the backup of the database seems not to save the blueprints and the drawings, it must save them"; "restoring a backup says 'this is not a backup file', clearly an error; both ways, the database and the backup copy, are clearly not finished").** **What was wrong.** (1) **A backup was cut off as it began.** Writing and restoring ran in the scope of the Settings screen. After the file picker the lock came up (D346), nothing of the app is composed while it is locked, the screen left the composition and its scope was cancelled — a few milliseconds after the file was made. What stayed on the disk was an empty or cut file, with no message; restoring it later said "This is not a Notio backup file", truthfully. The same happened at a turn of the phone during a backup, and to the file of the database. A restore cut in the same way could leave notes without their files. (2) **The file of the database was the SQLite file alone.** Drawings and blueprints (the picture and the vector layer), audio, photos, videos, the pictures laid over them, the backgrounds and the melodies of reminders are files beside the database, so none of them was in it. **Now.** (1) The four jobs — write a backup, restore one, export the file of the database, import one — run in the scope of the app (`AppContainer.appScope`, made for work that must finish when its screen is gone) and are only waited for by the screen: a lock, a turn of the phone or leaving Settings no longer ends them. With D346 the lock no longer comes up there at all. (2) **The file of the database is an archive** (`notio-database-<date>.db.zip`, format `notio-database` version 1): a marker, the database file as SQLite / SQLCipher wrote it, and every file of the folders `drawings`, `drawings-vector`, `audio`, `media`, `overlay_images`, `backgrounds`, `reminder_sounds`. Importing: the database is checked and put in place as before (a wrong password changes nothing), then the files of this phone are replaced by those of the archive; a name that would lead out of its folder is skipped. **A database file of an earlier version (the bare `.db`) is still imported**, as before, without files. The files in the archive are not encrypted, as they are not on the phone: with database encryption on, the archive protects the notes' text and not the pictures — the ZIP backup with a password protects everything. Only Android writes and reads this file (the computer has no such export), so nothing changes for a sync or for the backup format (rule 8). `RawDatabaseArchiveTest`. The words of the page (ten languages) still say "the database file": they are right, only shorter than the truth; changing them is left for the next pass of the texts. Windows §5: nothing. | Done 0.9.102 |
| D348 | **"Check the connections again" on the Sync page, and a found device is its name and address, not an icon (owner, 2026-10-10: "the search does not renew the network connections: mobile data or a VPN was on at the start, during the work Wi-Fi was turned on, and the data are still those of the old connection; a button to check the connections again beside the search of a device, there and there, a hint on the button, into the help, and the way from the hint into the help, as usual"; "when a device is found it shows a clock; beside it at least the name of the device and the IP address with the port, an icon alone says nothing — the screen Find a device, the icon of 'waits with a code'").** (1) **The addresses of this device, its announcement and the search were made once**, when the page (or the app, with "always ready") began to listen, and stayed those of the network of that moment. `SyncEngine.recheckNetwork()`: the addresses are read again, the device is announced again and the search starts anew with an empty list; the port, a code that is shown and a question that is asked stay. The button **Check the connections again** (`sync_recheck`, the icon Refresh by `ButtonIcons`) stands beside "Wait for a device" / "Find a device" and in the window Find a device above the list; its hint leads into the help of the sync (the key begins with `sync`), the help lists it with its icon (`HelpMap`) and says when to press it (`help_sync_body`, ten languages). It is a press, not a watch of the network: nothing runs for it. (2) **A found device in "Find a device" was drawn as one icon.** Its row is a button, and a button with words of a resource becomes an icon (D307) — here the words "waits with a code" made the clock, and the name and the address were hidden inside it. The row now keeps its words (it shows a name and a value, which rule 9 leaves as words): "<name>  (<address>:<port>)" and under it whether the device waits with a code. (3) **The code of a pairing has Copy beside it** (owner: "the code on the device that waits gets the classic icon Copy, to put it on the clipboard and pass it by another channel"; "the code itself can be selected too, not only copied by the icon"): the code stays selectable text, and the button `action_copy` puts it on the clipboard, marked as sensitive so the phone does not show it in the preview of the clipboard. Windows D123, the same on the computer (there the announcement lives per network address and is made anew). | Done 0.9.102 |
| D349 | **The Reminders are in the menu of the settings again (owner, 2026-10-10: "the reminders moved, that is fine, but why were they taken out of the menu of the gear; a submenu Reminders must be there too; through it we come to the same place, that was logical; and on the main screen through the icon too, as it is made now").** The settings of the reminders went to the Reminders page (D309, D318) and the menu of the settings lost its line. Now the menu has the line **Reminders** again, after "Notes" (the title `reminders_title`, the words under it `settings_cat_reminders_hint`, ten languages); it opens the same Reminders page as the bell of the main screen, which stays as it is. It is a way into a page, not a page of the settings, so `SettingsSection` is unchanged. Windows D124. | Done 0.9.102 |
| D350 | **The last resort: the letter of the recovery code also carries the password of the database and the password of the backups (owner, 2026-10-10: "at an attempt to get into a device where the password, the PIN was forgotten, the mail gets the current data for the entry, the password of the database and the password of the backup, so that everything can be recovered for sure; this is the last resort"; decided the same day: (1) the secret of the lock is kept only as a hash and cannot be sent — a one-time code and a new secret, as now: "yes, that will do"; (2) the two passwords go in the letter as plain text: "yes"; (3) only at the button "Forgot?", never by itself after wrong tries).** "Forgot?" → "Send a code to my e-mail" (D185) sends the six-digit code as before; the same letter now ends with the passwords kept on this device: the password of the encrypted database (when encryption is on) and the saved password of the backups (when one is saved, whether its switch is on or not). With them a copy of the database file and a protected backup can be opened on another device even if this one is lost to the lock. Nothing is sent without that press; the letter goes to the person's own address of the mail settings, over the connection chosen there. The page says so before the press ("the letter will also contain…"), the letter itself says to delete it afterwards, the help of the lock says what the letter carries (`help_lock_body`). The text is put together by `RecoveryMail.body` of the shared code (the same on the computer; `RecoveryMailTest`). **The risk, accepted by the owner:** whoever reads that mailbox gets both passwords; whoever holds the unlocked-by-nobody phone can make the letter be sent, but not read it. The words in ten languages (`lock_email_secrets_*`). Windows D126. | Done 0.9.103 |
| D351 | **A ring nobody answers lights the screen for less each time: 60 seconds, then 15, then 5 (owner, 2026-10-10: "yes, 15 seconds, and the third 5 seconds and on"; the question of D345).** A reminder that repeats — until Done, or with extra rings — lit the screen for a whole minute at every ring, with no end for "until Done": on a phone nobody is near, the largest cost a reminder has. Now the alarm screen holds the screen for `RingScreen.holdMillis`: the first ring of a series 60 s (as long as a melody plays), the second 15 s, the third and every later one 5 s; after that the phone's own timeout turns the screen off, as before. The melody, the notification and the alarm on the lock screen are as they were: only the time the screen is held changes. **Which ring it is** is counted on the phone (`RingSeries`, plain preferences, one line per reminder): a ring that comes within two repeat intervals of the last one goes on the series, any other starts a new one, and every answer — Done, Turn off, Snooze, opening it — ends the series, so the ring after a snooze is a first ring again. A reminder that comes back on another day starts at 60 s. Nothing of the reminder in the database changes, nothing travels in a backup or a sync. `RingScreenTest`. Windows: the computer does not light a screen for a reminder, nothing for §5. | Done 0.9.104 |
| D352 | **"Find a device" looks at the network first (owner, 2026-10-10: "the button to check the network and renew the data of the connection stays; and at a repeated press of Find the network should be checked too, the interface may have changed"; adds to D348).** A press of "Find a device" asks `SyncEngine.recheckNetworkIfChanged()`: when the addresses of this device are not the ones it listens with, the whole of D348 is done (addresses, announcement, search anew) before the window opens; when they are the same nothing is touched, so the list of devices already found stays and the window opens at once. The button "Check the connections again" does it whatever the addresses are. Windows D127. | Done 0.9.104 |
| D353 | **The work in the background by the rules of Android itself; the phone that waits for a sync is silent on the network (owner, 2026-10-10: "the most important thing is to work in the background in the best way and not to drain the battery; find the best solutions on the internet, they have to be applied"; the same written into FitEasy).** Read: the Android guides "Schedule alarms", "Optimize for Doze and App Standby", the reference of `NsdManager`, the pages and the posts of Android vitals on wake-ups and partial wake locks (the list with what each says is `docs/BATTERY_RU.md`). **Against them, point by point:** an alarm, not a timer or a service, for what happens at a time — so; an inexact alarm wherever the minute does not matter, an exact one only for what the person set to the minute — so (`setExactAndAllowWhileIdle`; the battery saver of D308 makes them inexact); `setAlarmClock` takes the phone out of Doze before every alarm and is the costly one — not used; no wake lock of our own — so; a foreground service only for what the person expects to go on now — the melody for its minute, a recording, and the wait for a sync that the person turned on; the search of the network stopped when the app is not on the screen — so since D345; no request to be let out of the battery optimisation — so. **One thing was not by the rules and is mended here:** while the phone waited for a sync in the background it still announced itself on the network (mDNS). An announcement keeps the phone a member of the multicast group, so the Wi-Fi hands it every such packet of the network and wakes it for each. Now the announcement too is withdrawn when Notio leaves the screen and made again when it comes back (`SyncEngine.lookForDevices` now means both); in the background only the port waits, which costs nothing until somebody connects. A device that is paired already reaches the phone by the address of the last sync (`Pairing.lastHost`), so a sync started from the computer works as before; when the address of the phone has changed since then, Notio has to be opened on the phone once. A new pairing is made with the app on the screen, as always. The words under the switch say so (`sync_always_hint_phone`, ten languages). **Known and left, with the reason:** a reminder that repeats more often than once in six minutes is more than the ten wake-ups an hour Android vitals call excessive — it is what the person asked of that reminder, and the screen is held for seconds from the second ring (D351); in Doze the phone delivers the alarms of one app no closer than nine minutes to each other, so such a reminder comes late on a phone that lies still — Android's own saving. The start for "the clock was set" stays until the journal (D345) shows how often a phone sends it. Not tried on a phone. | Done 0.9.104 |
| D354 | **A page "Rights" in the menu of the settings: every right of the app given and taken back in one place (owner, 2026-10-11: "make a tab Rights in the menu, move (double) there the giving of all the rights and leaves the app needs, and there too the taking back of each right and the taking back of all rights").** Settings → **Rights** (`SettingsSection.PERMISSIONS`, after "Security"): one card per right with what it is for, whether it is given, and Allow / Take back; under them "Take back all rights" and the way to the page of Notio in the phone's settings. **The rights:** notifications; exact alarms; the alarm over the lock screen (Android 14+); showing over other apps; the microphone; the camera; the place. A right this Android does not know (notifications before 13, exact alarms before 12) is not listed: the phone gives it to every app. **Giving:** a right the app may ask for is asked for by the phone's own question — and when the phone asks no more (refused twice), the page of the app in the settings opens; a leave that only the settings can change opens its own page there. **Taking back:** Android lets an app give up a right it asked for (`revokeSelfPermissionsOnKill`, Android 13+), and does it when the app is closed — the card says "goes when Notio is closed" until then, and Allow brings it back; a leave of the settings, and on Android 12 and older every right, is taken back on the page of the settings that the button opens. "All rights": what the app can give up itself is given up at once, and the page of Notio in the settings opens for the rest. **Doubled, not moved:** every function still asks for its right when it needs it (the reminders, a recording, the camera), and the pages that showed a right (the battery, the reminders) keep theirs; a right taken back here is asked for again by the function that needs it. Nothing is asked for at the start of the app, as before. The help has the section "Rights" (`help_permissions_*`), a hint of the page leads there (keys `perm_*`); ten languages. `AppRightTest`. Windows D130: nothing to give or take there. | Done 0.9.105 |
| D355 | **The name of the app in the system follows the language of the system: "Нотио от Вибик" in Russian, "Notio by vibic" in every other (owner, 2026-10-11: "the name is in Russian there; can we make it depend on the system, Russian or English — Notio by Vibic, Нотио от Вибик? I mean the name of the app in the operating systems"; adds to D228, D299).** The label under the icon of the launcher, in the list of apps and in the phone's settings is `@string/app_label`, a text of its own in `values/app_label.xml` and `values-ru/app_label.xml` — the phone takes it by ITS language, not by the language chosen inside Notio. Inside the app the name stays "Notio by vibic" (`app_name`: the title of the main screen, the name in an authenticator), as do the package and the files of a delivery. Only Russian has a name of its own; the other eight languages of the app show the English one. The English name keeps its small "v" (D228), as everywhere in the project. Windows D131, Linux D13. | Done 0.9.105 |
| D356 | **A check of the whole code for safety; an archive of the database is unpacked within limits (owner, 2026-10-11: "check the whole code for safety, that we used no dangerous libraries and add no vulnerabilities to the phone").** **Looked at:** every library of both apps with the ones they bring (17 outside AndroidX and Kotlin on the phone, 13 on the desktop) against the OSV database of known vulnerabilities — none known for the versions in use; the Gradle wrapper jar against the checksums Gradle publishes (the genuine one of 8.14); the repositories (Google and Maven Central only); the tracked files for keys and passwords (none); the manifest (one exported activity, one exported receiver that acts on five system broadcasts only, no cleartext flag, no backup to the cloud); every `PendingIntent` (all immutable); the sync (keys, frames, limits, the local network only), the mail client (certificate and name checked), the lock, the Keystore box, the backups and imports (names, sizes), the journal. No WebView, no code loaded at run time, no Java serialization, no trust-all TLS, no world-readable file. **Found and mended:** an archive of the database (D347) was unpacked without any limit — a made-up file of a few kilobytes could fill the storage of the phone. Now the database is read up to 4 GB, a file up to 2 GB (a bigger one is left out), at most 200 000 files, and the reading ends when less than 100 MB of storage is free; a database that does not fit is not taken and nothing of the phone is changed. **Found and written down for the owner (`SECURITY_REVIEW.md` O13 - O16, `docs/TODO_RU.md` §2):** a paired device that says "everything is set beforehand" is not asked here, a replacement included (D322, D333); the letter of "Forgot?" carries the passwords also through a mail server without encryption (D350); another app can open Notio with the action of a notification (the lock still stands); the journal is mirrored to the system log. **Not done:** a test on a device, a penetration test, a review by a third party. | Done 0.9.106 |
| D357 | **The port this device listens on can be set by hand (owner, 2026-10-11: "by hand it must be possible to set it too; automatic by default, but the manual way must be there, and it must say whether the port is taken or not").** Until now the phone always tried 47821 and took any free port when it was taken; only the port of the OTHER device could be typed (D280). **Now:** the card of this device on the Sync page has a line of the port and a button "Port of this device…". By default the port is chosen automatically, as before — and when 47821 is taken the line says so and names the port in use. A port of one's own is 1024 - 65535 (lower ones belong to the system and cannot be opened by an app); while it is typed the dialog tries it off the UI thread and says "free" or "taken by another program". A taken port may still be kept (the program that has it may be closed later). **A port chosen by hand is never replaced silently:** when it cannot be opened the device does not listen, the page says so in the colour of an error, the journal of the sync has a line, and nothing else is opened. `SyncStore.listenPort` (0 — automatic; kept in the private preferences of the sync, not in a backup), `SyncEngine.portBusy`, `SyncEngine.isPortFree`, `SyncAddress.listenPort`. Paired devices learn the new port from the announcement on the network; one that reaches this device by the address of the last sync needs the port corrected with Address… — the dialog says it. The protocol, the keys and the local-network-only rule are untouched. Nothing new runs in the background: the port is opened where it was opened before (`docs/BATTERY_RU.md` unchanged). Hint, icon (`Tune`), help text and help list in ten languages (rule 11). Tests: the chosen port is the one listened on; a taken one is said and no other opened; back to automatic; the limits of the number. Not run on a phone. | Done 0.9.107 |
| D358 | **A tick "completed" on a task — on its card, in View and in Edit — with a filter, a search and a sort by it (owner, 2026-10-11: "on tasks there must be everywhere, in the preview, in the view and in the editing, a switch completed, as a tick; add also a filter by completed or not, a search by this value and a sort by it").** **What the tick is:** the task has a status that ends it (D337: Done, Closed, Cancelled) — the `done` every Notio has always written, so nothing of the format changes and an older Notio shows the same tick. **Ticking** makes the task Done and keeps the status it had in a new optional field `before` of `TaskDocument`; **clearing** gives that status back (In work when there is none: a task of an older Notio, or one that never had another status). Choosing an ending status from the list keeps `before` the same way; an older Notio ignores the field and drops it when it saves — the tick then gives back In work. `TaskDoneBox` stands first in `TaskStateLine`, so the card, View and Edit have it at once; in the trash it is only shown. **Filter:** two chips with words before the chips of the statuses, "Not completed" and "Completed", with how many tasks each has above a folder of tasks and without numbers in the search; one of them or none (`TaskFilter.matches(note, states, done)`); they work together with the chips of the statuses. **Search:** `SearchQuery.taskDone` — the same two chips in the search of notes; a chosen one makes a search by itself, as a status does, and narrows a search by words. **Sort:** `SortKey.DONE` in the keys of a folder of tasks, "Completed": the open tasks first, the completed after, what is not a task last both ways. The choices of the filter are kept while the app runs, as the chips of the statuses are. Hint and help in ten languages (rule 11): the tick has the hint "Completed" and leads into the help of tasks; the chips are words (rule 9). The string `sort_key_done` (the sort of list lines, "Done / not done") is another thing and is left as it is: the new key is `sort_key_completed`. Tests: the tick and the status given back, the filter, the search, the sort (both platforms); on the desktop the tick and the chip pressed in the running window. Not run on a phone. | Done 0.9.108 |
| D359 | **The two chips of the filter by the tick are icons; a budget has Delete in View, and a deletion is put back from its log (owner, 2026-10-12: "replace completed and not completed with icons with hints (as usual, with the way into the help) in the filter, the words take too much room. In budget notes — make an icon delete on the elements in View, a logged deletion of course, that can be restored in the log").** **Filter (D358 changed):** the chips "Not completed" and "Completed" are an empty box and a ticked box (`AppIcons.CheckBoxBlank`, `AppIcons.CheckBox`), with the number of tasks beside it above a folder; the words are the hint of the chip and what a screen reader says, and a press on the hint opens the help at "tasks" — as the chips of the statuses beside them (D343). The texts `task_filter_open` / `task_filter_done` stay. **Delete in View:** every item and every group of a budget (not the total, not the row of a group total) has the bin, red, with the hint "Delete" that leads to the help of budgets; in Edit Delete stays in the ⋮ menu. It asks first, as before, and the question now says that the log can put it back. **The deletion in the log:** the `DELETED` line knows its node (`nodeId`) and keeps what was deleted — the item, or the group with everything inside — in its `detail`: `TREE` or nothing as before, then a line break and a `DeletedRecord` (JSON, the nodes as `MergeRecord.Node`). No new column and no new format of the backup or of the sync: `detail` is free text; an older Notio shows such a line as "Deleted" and cannot restore it. **Restore:** `BudgetEditor.restoreDeleted(entry, all)` puts the nodes back as they were — the same ids (so the earlier lines of the item can be undone again), names, sums, goals, quantities, comments, the place among the brothers — into the group they were in, or under the total when that group is gone; the totals are counted again; one write. A line of its own, `RESTORED` (`detail` = the id of the `DELETED` line), so a deletion is restored once; the line then says "Restored". Refused, with words, when the item is in the budget already (Undo of the note brought it back) or the record is not one whole tree. Lines made before this version kept nothing and have no Restore. Not asked first: a restore destroys nothing and is deleted again by the bin. **Help and hints (rule 11):** `help_tasks_body` and `help_budget_body` say it in ten languages; the icons are in `HelpMap` (tasks: the two boxes; budget: the bin and Restore). **Tests:** `BudgetLogRestoreTest` (an item and a group with things inside come back with their values and totals, into the total when the group is gone, once only, not when it is there already, an old line cannot), `BudgetLogTest` (the line of a deletion). Not looked at on a phone. | DONE 0.9.109 |
| D360 | **The message at the bottom of the screen goes by a swipe and by a time the person sets (owner, 2026-10-13: "after the trash is emptied a notice hangs at the bottom for long and cannot be swiped away; let such notices be swiped, limit them by time, 10 seconds by default, and give a setting from 0 to 60 seconds, 0 - off").** Every snackbar of the app goes through one place, `core/ui/Notices.kt`: `SnackbarHostState.showNotice(text, actionLabel)` shows the message for `NoticeTime.seconds` (the shared `shared/src/com/notio/common/NoticeTime.kt`: 0..60, default 10) by its own timer - Material's own duration is not used, since the system's accessibility time stretched it - and a newer message takes the place of the one shown; `NoticeHost` draws the snackbar inside a swipe-to-dismiss, to either side. **0 = no message is shown at all**, the one with "Undo" too (what was deleted stays in the trash; the setting's hint says so). The setting is Settings -> General -> "Messages at the bottom of the screen", a slider 0..60 s, kept on this device only (`SettingsDataStore.noticeSeconds`; not in the backup - the format of the backup does not change). System notifications (reminders) and Android's own toasts are not these messages. Help: "settings". | Done 0.9.110 |
| D361 | **Delete on a task: an icon on its card and in View (owner, 2026-10-13: "an icon Delete on a task in the preview and in View").** The line of a task - the tick, the status, the priority - ends with the red bin where the task can be changed: on its card (`TaskCardLine`, not in the trash) and in View (`TaskView`); not in Edit. The bin asks the same question as "Delete" of the note's menu ("... will be moved to the trash") and the note goes to the trash, from where it is restored. On the card that is the main screen's own dialog (`HomeDialog.DeleteNote`, with "Undo" in the message after); in View the note's screen had no deletion at all - `NoteEditorViewModel.deleteNote` saves what is typed, moves the note to the trash and the screen goes back. Both come through `LocalTaskDelete`; without it (the trash) there is no bin. Hint "Delete", help "tasks". | Done 0.9.110 |
| D362 | **Copy: an icon of two sheets in the preview and in View of every kind that has text or a picture (owner, 2026-10-13: "in the preview of an MD note a Copy button, two sheets as is the fashion, and if it is a list - on every item; the same in the preview of a plain note; on list notes copy an item; on a budget and a task too; on a drawing and a blueprint copy to the clipboard as well").** One icon, `CopyIcon` (`core/ui/CopyIcon.kt`: `ContentCopy`, hint "Copy", help "share"), and one rule of what it copies. **Note and MD note:** the icon over the text copies the whole text - in the preview of the editor (the "View" tab) and on the card; a line of a list inside the text (`- `, `* `, `1. `, a checkbox line) has its own icon after it, which copies the words of that line without the marker. **List:** every row has the icon in View, it copies the row as a line of text (name, quantity, unit, price, total, comment - what the row has). **Budget:** every row has the icon in View, it copies the name with the sum, the goal and the remainder as shown. **Task:** the icon in the line of the task, on the card and in View, copies the name, the status, the dates, the comment and the description. **Drawing and blueprint:** the icon in View and on the card copies the picture itself to the clipboard (PNG; on Android through the app's FileProvider, the file in the cache folder `shared_images`, replaced by the next copy). Cards: one icon at the right edge for a note, an MD note, a list, a budget, a drawing and a blueprint (a task has it in its line); it copies the whole note as "Share" hands it over - the text with the title, or the picture. Photo, video, audio and schedule cards have none, nor has the compact list. After a copy "Copied" is said (a toast; Android 13+ shows its own clipboard panel instead, so nothing more is said there). What is copied is plain text, as the person sees it: no hidden text goes to the clipboard (D363). Android 13+ shows its own clipboard preview. | Done 0.9.110 |
| D363 | **A note can hide its content until the eye is pressed (owner, 2026-10-13: "if 'hide' is on in Edit, then an eye, as on a budget; on all kinds of notes - blueprint, drawing, list; not on photo, video, audio"; where the mark is kept - owner, the same day: "in the note itself", asked whether the migration runs at an update - it does).** `Note.hidden` is a new column of the table `note` (`hidden INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`): **schema 14**, `MIGRATION_13_14` adds the column at the first start after the update, every note so far is not hidden. The mark travels with the note: `NoteRecord.hidden` in the backup, in a sync and in a clone - an optional field, a file without it is read as before, and an older Notio skips it (`ignoreUnknownKeys`), so there the note is simply shown; the version of the backup format does not change. A note file (export of one note) carries the field, an import of one note file starts not hidden. **Kinds:** note (Word), MD note, list, drawing, blueprint (`HIDEABLE_KINDS`); not photo, video, screen recording, audio; a budget keeps its own hiding of amounts (D272); a task and a schedule were not asked for. **Edit:** the crossed eye in the row of the View / Edit tabs turns the option on and off (`NoteUseCases.setHidden`; tinted while on; hint "Hide the content" / "Do not hide the content"). **View:** with the option on the note opens with "The content is hidden" instead of its content, and the eye in the tabs row (or the button under the words) shows it until the note is closed; pressed again it hides it. **Card:** the preview (the lines of text, the picture of a drawing) is replaced by "The content is hidden", and the eye at the right edge shows it for this card until pressed again or the list is left - as the eye of a budget does (D306). Copy (D362) is not offered while the content is hidden. One place for all kinds: `core/ui/NoteHide.kt` (`NoteHide`, `LocalNoteHide`, `HideEye`, `HiddenContent`, `HiddenLine`); `ViewEditTabs` draws the eye, each editor shows `HiddenContent` in its View. **What it is not:** it is a cover against a glance over the shoulder, not a lock - the title, the tags and the comment stay visible, the search still finds the words of a hidden note and opens it covered, Share / Export / the backup hand the content over as before, and the app lock (D124) is what keeps a stranger out. Help: "privacy". | Done 0.9.111 |
| D364 | **Hide the content of several notes at once (owner, 2026-10-14: "a mass choice of notes in a folder - there is one for deleting - and turn hiding on or off for the chosen").** The bar of the "Select" mode of a folder (D167) gets two icons next to Delete: the crossed eye "Hide the content (N)" and the eye "Do not hide the content (N)". N counts the picked notes the press would change: of the `HIDEABLE_KINDS` (D363) and not yet hidden / hidden; photo, video, audio, a budget, a task and a schedule among the picks are left as they are, and an icon with N = 0 is greyed. Each changed note goes through `NoteUseCases.setHidden`, so "modified" moves as for one note. No question first (nothing is lost, the other icon turns it back); the notice "Content hidden: N" / "Content no longer hidden: N" has Undo, which turns back only the notes this press changed. The mode stays open with the same picks. The media screens (D146) share `SelectionBar` and show no eye. Hints `privacy_hide_selected` / `privacy_show_selected` lead to the help "privacy" (`IconAction` takes a `topic`); its text tells about it. | Done 0.9.114 |
| D293 | **A ringing reminder can always be stopped (user, 2026-10-01: "a bug: if a reminder went off and at the same time the system alarm clock, the Android alarm covered the reminder and the reminder's sound kept playing, no way to stop it; in the notification area make a Stop button while the reminder rings, plus in the Reminders menu an indicator of the reminder ringing now, like a red light, and next to it a button with a fitting Stop icon; write the same requirements into Notio Windows and Linux").** Cause: the melody of a reminder with its own volume or fade-in is played by `ReminderRingService` (D130), which did not take the audio focus, so it played on under the phone's alarm clock and after it; its notification had only Snooze and Done / Turn off (the Silence action had been dropped), and the app showed nothing about a ringing reminder. Now: (1) the service holds the audio focus as an alarm (`AUDIOFOCUS_GAIN_TRANSIENT`, `USAGE_ALARM`): another alarm or a call -> the melody waits; the sound back -> it goes on within the same 60 s; lost for good -> it ends, the notification stays; (2) the notification of a ringing melody has **Stop sound** first (`ACTION_SILENCE`: the sound ends, the reminder stays as it is — still to be confirmed, snoozed or turned off); (3) `ReminderRingService.ringing` says which reminder rings; the Reminders page shows at the top a red pulsing light, "Ringing now: <title>" and a Stop sound icon button (VolumeOff), and the card of that reminder the same light and button. A reminder rung by its channel (100 % volume, no fade-in) plays its sound once and needs no Stop. (0.9.81, owner: "did you take the vibration into account too? ... I would even say just Stop": the button is **Stop** (not Stop sound) and also cancels any vibration of the app; the notification's own vibration is one short burst when it appears and never repeats.) | DONE 0.9.80 (`ReminderRingService`, `ReminderNotifier`, `RemindersScreen`, `AppIcons.VolumeOff`; strings `reminder_stop_sound`, `reminder_ringing_now` in 10 languages; `ReminderRingTest` (2); not run on a device) |
| D292 | **A themed (monochrome) launcher icon (lint MonochromeLauncherIcon; owner, 2026-10-01: "finish the plans").** Android 13+ tints the icon in one colour when the person turns on themed icons; with only the coloured foreground the white sheet and its blue lines became one blob. `ic_launcher_monochrome`: the sheet as an outline, its lines, "vibic" and "777", the same geometry; in both adaptive icons. | DONE 0.9.79 (`res/drawable/ic_launcher_monochrome.xml`, `mipmap-anydpi/ic_launcher*.xml`; not seen on a device) |
| D291 | **Translation sources in step with the xml (T1).** The tsv sources of be, es, it, zh, ja, ko lagged the generated xml (help texts and 58 strings edited or added straight in the xml), so `gen_lang.py` would have rolled six languages back. `sync_tsv_from_xml.py` now also writes changed texts into the winning tsv line (the last file that has the key) in place; `gen_lang.py --check` writes nothing and says whether a rebuild gives exactly the current xml. All six pass; `translations/README.md` makes `--check` the rule before and after any string change. | DONE 0.9.79 (`translations/gen_lang.py`, `translations/sync_tsv_from_xml.py`, the tsv files; the xml unchanged in content) |
| D290 | **The lock wait cannot be ended by setting the time (O12).** A wait after wrong tries was counted by the wall clock only. Now it is also kept on the clock since boot (`SystemClock.elapsedRealtime`, which the person cannot set): `AppLockConfig.blockedAtElapsed`, `blockedWait`. The wait ends when both clocks say so: moving the time forward does not help, moving it back before the start begins it again; after a reboot (the clock since boot is smaller than at the start) only the wall clock counts — a reboot costs time and the phone's own unlock. The lock screen asks `waitingUntil()` for its countdown. Older configs (no `blockedWait`) behave as before. | DONE 0.9.79 (`AppLockUseCases(elapsed)`, `AppContainer`, `LockScreen`; `AppLockUseCasesTest` +3) |
| D289 | **R8 in release builds (O8).** `isMinifyEnabled` and `isShrinkResources` on; `-dontobfuscate` and `-keepattributes SourceFile,LineNumberTable`, so the stack traces of the error window (D224) and the app journal (D230) stay readable for the owner to send (the source is open: renaming would hide nothing). The libraries bring their own rules (kotlinx.serialization, Room, SQLCipher's JNI, lifecycle, Media3, CameraX); the app's code uses no reflection; R8 reported no missing rules. The release APK goes from about 55 MB to about 19 MB. Not run by the agent (no emulator with a phone image here): the owner's first check of the release APK is the real test; the debug APK is built without R8. targetSdk stays 36: the behaviour changes of 37 apply only on Android 17, which neither the owner's phone nor BlueStacks runs, so nobody could check them; raise it with such a device. | DONE 0.9.79 (`app/build.gradle.kts`, `app/proguard-rules.pro`) |
| D288 | **Sync: a short handshake and the free storage Android can give (code review 2026-10-01, point 5).** Connections are answered one at a time; one that did not finish its handshake could hold the others up to 5 minutes. The handshake now has 15 s (`HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT`), a known device gets the long timeout after it. The count of wrong pairing codes and the announcement are safe from two threads (`AtomicInteger`, `@Synchronized`). The free storage of D284 is `StorageManager.getAllocatableBytes` (the cache Android clears on demand counts), falling back to the plain free space. | DONE 0.9.78 (`SyncEngine`, `AppContainer.allocatableBytes`; not run on a device) |
| D287 | **The lock keeps the rounds of its hashes (O4).** `AppLockConfig.iterations`, `answerIterations`, `emailCodeIterations`: a lock saved before 0.9.78 has none and reads as 210 000 (what it was made with; `CredentialHasher.LEGACY_ITERATIONS`, never changed). A right try with a hash of other rounds than today's makes it again with today's while the secret is at hand, so the default can be raised in any version without anyone's lock breaking. A stored count outside 1 - 10 000 000 is refused (a damaged file must not hang the check). The default stays 210 000: each unlock pays it, and raising it should follow a measurement on the owner's phone. | DONE 0.9.78 (`CredentialHasher`, `AppLock`, `AppLockUseCases`; `AppLockUseCasesTest` +3) |
| D286 | **The backup manifest is streamed and limited by the memory (code review 2026-10-01, point 2).** The manifest (folders, every note's text and vector drawing, budgets, logs) was read as bytes, then text, then objects: three copies, up to 256 MB a file. It is now decoded straight from the archive (`decodeFromStream`) and written straight into it (`encodeToStream`); the limit is a quarter of the memory Android gives the app, between 32 and 256 MB (`BackupCodec.manifestLimit`; a fixed 32 MB was considered and rejected: a person with many large drawings has a legitimately big manifest). Over it, or out of memory while reading: the new error `TOO_LARGE`, "too large to open with this device's memory", instead of a crash. | DONE 0.9.78 (`BackupCodec`, string `transfer_error_too_large` in 10 languages; `BackupCodecTest` +2) |
| D285 | **Back tapped twice left an empty grey window (user, 2026-10-01: "if I press the arrow to leave a note and right away the same area top left where the app's name is, the app hangs: just a grey background until restarted; I reproduce it easily on the phone").** Cause: the note's arrow saves and then pops; while the note is leaving it is still drawn and takes taps, so the second tap (on its arrow, where the main screen's title comes) popped the main screen too and the back stack was empty. Every navigation action of a screen now runs only while that screen is on top (`NoteBudgetNavHost.from(entry)`): the second tap is ignored; the same guards a double tap that opened a note twice. | DONE 0.9.78 (`NoteBudgetNavHost`; `AppFlowTest.aDoubleTapOnBackInANoteLeavesTheMainScreenShown` fails without the fix: no main screen) |
| D284 | **Sync does not fill the phone (user, 2026-10-01, after the code review: "think how to take this into account").** A receiving device keeps at least **200 MB** free (`SyncSession.SPACE_RESERVE`) beyond the size of what came: the import copies the files out of the received archive once more. A device asked to receive with less than that refuses at once (`decision`, reason `no-space`); a device starting a sync that would receive checks before it connects; while the data comes, every 8 MB the free storage is checked against what came so far plus the reserve, and the stream stops with `no-space`: the half-received file is deleted, nothing is imported, the next sync works. The message names the receiving device and the 200 MB. The sender does not announce the size (protocol v1 stays), so a too-big transfer is stopped while it comes, not before. | DONE 0.9.77 (`SyncSession.receiveData(freeBytes)`, `SyncEngine(freeBytes)`, `SyncReasons.NO_SPACE`, string `sync_err_no_space` in 10 languages; `SyncEngineTest` +1; not run on a device) |
| D283 | **The lock checks one try at a time, off the main thread (code review 2026-10-01, F6 in `SECURITY_REVIEW.md`).** PBKDF2 with 210 000 rounds ran on the main thread (the screen froze at every try), and quick repeated taps ran checks side by side: each read the same count of wrong tries, so some were lost and the waits or the opt-in wipe after N tries could be got around. Now the stretching runs on `Dispatchers.Default` and the checks of the secret, the security answer, the mail code and the authenticator code hold one mutex. Also: `BackupCodec.read` (the whole archive in memory, used only by tests) removed; the tests read through the streaming `readManifest` / `AttachmentReader` the app uses. Planned from the same review: O12 (the wait is counted by the wall clock), O8 (R8). | DONE 0.9.77 (`AppLockUseCases.checking`; `AppLockUseCasesTest` +1, fails without the fix) |
| D282 | **Undo an operation from a budget's log (user, 2026-09-30: "the log in the budget and in income, expense: be able to undo an operation; if I made a mistake I can undo it, as it is done for the merge").** A line of the log of any budget (account, income, expense) that changed an item — its **sum, goal, quantity, name** — or **added an item or group** has **Undo**, asked first. A change is taken back by **its own difference** (the value now minus what that line added), so later changes stay; a rename gives the old name back; an added item / group is deleted again with what is in it now (the dialog says so, the button is red). The undo is a line of its own ("Undone: …", kind `OP_UNDONE`, detail = the undone line's id and kind); a line is undone once and then says "Undone". Moves and deletions are not undone here (the Undo button of the note covers the recent ones; a deletion keeps no data to bring back). For this every new line knows its item: `budget_log.node_id`, **schema 13** (migration 12 -> 13), in backups and sync (`BudgetLogRecord.nodeId`); lines made before, and lines of a copy imported with new ids, have none and show no Undo. | DONE 0.9.76 (`BudgetEditor.undoLogEntry` / `canUndo` / `undone`, `LogLine.nodeId`, `BudgetLogEntity.nodeId`, `AppDatabase.MIGRATION_12_13`, `BudgetLogScreen`; 9 strings and the budget help in 10 languages; `BudgetLogUndoTest` (4), `MigrationTest` +1; not run on a device) |
| D281 | **The tool panel of a drawing / blueprint on any side (user, 2026-09-30: "add the tool panel at the top and on the left in blueprints and drawings; now it is on the right and at the bottom; let it be any way").** The dock icon of the panel opens a menu of four: **bottom, right, top, left** (each with its picture, the chosen one ticked); the choice is kept (`drawing_tools_side`; the old right / bottom choice carries over). The sheet lies in the part the panel leaves free: `SheetFrame` knows `coveredTop` / `coveredLeft`, the free part starts after them, Fit, the limits of moving and the zoom steps use it; the fold arrow points to the panel's side. | DONE 0.9.76 (`ToolPanelSide`, `SheetFrame`, `SheetView`, `SettingsDataStore.drawingToolsSide`, `DrawingScreen`; 3 strings and the help in 10 languages; `SheetViewTest` +2, `AppFlowTest` switches sides by the menu; not run on a device) |
| D280 | **Correct the address of a paired device (user, 2026-09-30: "a bug: over Wi-Fi the phone is at .152 one day and .153 another, so the sync is lost and has to be set up again; on the syncs make it possible to edit the IP address and port; once a connection was made it can always be corrected; that is safe, the key stays set, nobody knows it?").** Cause: a paired device is looked for on the network by its id (mDNS); where that fails (many routers) the last address is used, which may be old. Now every kept connection shows **"Address: 192.168.1.153:47821"** and has **Address…**: IP (or a host name) and port, checked (IPv4 parts 0 - 255 or a plain name, port 1 - 65535). Only where to knock changes: the pairing key stays sealed on the phone, both sides still prove they know it (mutual HMAC over the handshake) and the data is encrypted with it, so a device at that address without the key is refused ("unknown pairing") and gets nothing — the answer to the owner's question is yes, it is safe. Written to the sync log. | DONE 0.9.76 (`SyncAddress`, `SyncSettings.AddressDialog`; 9 strings and the sync help in 10 languages; `SyncAddressTest` (2); not run on a device) |
| D279 | **A budget opens as it was left, groups open or closed (user, 2026-09-30: "open the budget note in the state it was left in, expanded or collapsed: if I closed it all collapsed, open it the same").** Checked: the open / closed mark of every group is stored in the database (`budget_node.is_expanded`) by the arrow of a group and by Collapse / Expand all, and read back when the note opens (`BudgetFoldingTest` with the real database: collapsed stays collapsed). Collapse all keeps the total open on purpose (D183: a closed budget still shows its top-level groups). Groups show open while the search of the note finds something in them (D238), without being saved so. So no fault was found in the code; the owner is asked what exactly opens differently (which note, after what: sync, a merge, the search, the total). | OPEN — asked the owner (0.9.76: `BudgetFoldingTest` added as evidence) |
| D278 | **Icons instead of words where they help (user, 2026-09-28: "can we cover the most with icons? where are the buttons with names?"; approved the same day: "your plan suits me, as you proposed do not remove the dangerous ones, and the long press is great too; update the help, the documentation, the TODO of Windows and Linux").** An audit of the text buttons (most in Settings, drawing, reminders, sync, budget, media, trash) split them: **icons** for frequent actions with a known symbol in crowded rows; **words kept** for dialog buttons (Save / Cancel / Delete / Create), rare or irreversible actions (Empty trash, resets, "Open settings", "Allow", Undo merge), buttons that show a value (the currency, the sort, seconds, recurrence) and the Settings rows. Every icon button (`IconAction`) has its name for TalkBack and as a tooltip on a long press. Built: **budget** line — log (clock with an arrow), display options (sliders, in Edit), collapse / expand all (arrows together / apart); the currency and the sort keep their words; the search's scroll offset follows (the display options no longer have their own line); **audio / photo / video / screen** — Select (checklist), play / pause of a clip (round ▶ / ⏸), full screen (corners); **selection bar** — select all, clear, delete the chosen (still asks); **trash** rows — restore (arrow out of the bin), save to a file (arrow down), delete for good (bin with a cross, still asks); **reminder card** — Done (tick), Edit (pencil), Cancel (crossed bell), Delete (bin), both still ask; **tags / units / currencies** — Rename (pencil) beside the cross; **list** — add item (list with a plus), add total by unit (Σ); **schedule** — add a time (clock with a plus), add a calculator, add a comment (bubble with a plus); **drawing / blueprint** — edit text, delete, duplicate, one step forward / back, to the front / back, a layer's shown / hidden (eye) and locked / free (lock), add a layer. The Stop of a running recording stays a big button with its word. The help says which icon is which on every screen, and that a long press shows the name. The icons are copied into `core/ui/AppIcons.kt` (path data of Material Icons 1.7.8, Apache 2.0, in `NOTICE.md`): the whole `material-icons-extended` library made the release APK 22 MB bigger (55 -> 77 MB) because the release is not shrunk by R8, so it was taken out again. | DONE 0.9.75 (`core/ui/IconAction`, the screens above; help in 10 languages; `AppFlowTest` finds the icons by their names; not run on a device) |
| D277 | **Rename a unit and a currency of one's own (user, 2026-09-28: "units of measurement added through the settings cannot be renamed, like tags for example — renaming must be made"; "the same with currencies: in the settings, the list of currencies, the added currencies must be renamable, like units and tags").** Settings → Units and Settings → Currencies get **Rename** next to each unit / currency the person added (built-in ones cannot change, as before). The rules of a new one apply (not empty, the length limit — 24 for a unit, 8 for a currency — not the name of another one or of a built-in one, case ignored; the unit / currency may take its own name in another case); a refused name keeps the dialog open with the reason. Lists refer to a unit by its id, so they show the new name at once (the copy a list keeps for another phone is written anew on its next save). A budget keeps the text of its currency in itself (D170), so renaming a currency rewrites that text in every budget using it, trashed ones included, all other settings kept, the budgets' "changed" time not touched. | DONE 0.9.74 (`UnitUseCases.rename`, `CurrencyUseCases.rename`, `BudgetCurrency.withLabel`, `UnitsViewModel` / `UnitsScreen`, `CurrenciesViewModel` / `CurrenciesScreen`; 4 strings and the settings help in 10 languages; `RenameCatalogTest` (3); not run on a device) |

| D274 | **TalkBack for people with weak sight — a plan (user, 2026-09-28: "think through how to bring in TalkBack for the visually impaired; sync the TODO to Notio Windows and Linux, do not forget").** PLANNED, nothing built but the labels of D270. Audit of 0.9.72: 54 icon buttons, 17 `contentDescription = null` (most beside a text, decorative), 35 `clickable` rows, 70 `semantics` blocks already, 7 files with raw `pointerInput` (drawing sheet, zoom, drag, calendar swipe). The plan, in steps a person can test with TalkBack on: (1) **labels** — every icon-only button and every symbol (▾ ▸ of budget groups, − % + zoom, 🔔 💰 📋 of the calendar, ⏰ of the alarm) gets words; toggles say on / off (`Role.Switch`, `stateDescription`); (2) **cards read as one** — a note / folder card merges its lines into one announcement (kind, name, pinned, reminder, tags, comment, preview) with custom actions for its ⋮ menu (open, pin, move, colour, share, delete) instead of hunting small buttons; (3) **headings and order** — section titles marked `heading()`, traversal order of the tab row, zoom and panels fixed; (4) **gestures have buttons** — drag to reorder gets "Move up / down" actions, swipes of the calendar and pinch zoom already have buttons (check they are reachable), the drawing sheet says what it is and that it is drawn with a finger (not made accessible itself); (5) **live changes announced** — snackbars, search counters, recording time, budget totals after a change (`liveRegion` polite); (6) **sizes and contrast** — touch targets ≥ 48 dp where they are 36 - 40 now, text contrast of D273 checked in both themes, font sizes up to the largest system one without cuts; (7) a **TalkBack pass** on a phone per screen, results in §22. Open question for step 2 (asked when it starts): whether a card's custom actions replace the ⋮ button for TalkBack or come in addition. Desktop: Windows Narrator / NVDA, Linux Orca (Compose Desktop accessibility) — the same list. | PLANNED (0.9.72: the zoom buttons of D270 have their words) |
| D273 | **Stronger card colours (user, 2026-09-28: "the colours are too pale, the background design of the same notes must be more exact").** The card of a note showed 14 % of its colour over the page, a folder card 22 %. On a strong colour the text turns light or dark by itself (`readableOn`: white below a luminance of 0.18, where both give the same contrast) — a note card switches the theme's `onSurface` / `onSurfaceVariant` for all its lines. Kept in the settings backup. | DONE 0.9.72 (`CardTint`, `SettingsDataStore.cardTint`, `SettingsRecord.cardTint`, `LocalCardTint`, `ReadableOn`, `NoteCard`, `FolderCard`, `SettingsScreen.CardTintOptions`; `ReadableOnTest` (2); not run on a device) |

Hidden, they show as `*****` on the card of the note, in the note (every row, the per-piece line, the Change dialog) and in its log; the **remainder** (goal − sum) is hidden when either is, and a hidden negative remainder is not red. | D260 | **The size of the sheet (user, 2026-09-27: "the matter is also the field: make it possible to change the size of the field — i.e. edit the size and set it in pixels or, by choice, cm, mm; any ratio: 16 by 9, or 9 by 16, 4 by 3 and 3 by 4, and whatever the user chooses by typing the width and height, ok?").** Plan: every drawing and blueprint has its own sheet size (today fixed 1080 x 1440, 3:4); a "Sheet size" dialog in Edit: presets 16:9, 9:16, 4:3, 3:4 (and the current), or width x height typed in px, cm or mm (cm / mm at a fixed 150 pixels per inch, the pixels shown); a limit on the pixels so the phone does not run out of memory; changing the size keeps the picture at the top left (a bigger sheet adds white, a smaller one cuts) after a confirmation, and Undo brings it back; the size travels in the note (backup, sync, export). | DONE 0.9.65 (`SheetSize` / `SheetUnit` (px, mm, cm at 150 ppi; 64..4096 a side, 4 MP in all; presets), `VectorDocument.sheetWidth / sheetHeight / sheetUnit` (defaults 1080 x 1440, "px": old files unchanged; backup, sync and the structured file carry it), `DrawingRenderer.newSheet / decodeToSheet / flatten` with a size and `resized` (top left kept), `DrawingViewModel.sheetSize`, `resizeSheet` (the raster undo steps merged, the view back to 1x), exports and the card preview in the sheet's proportions, `SheetSizeDialog` (ready ratios, unit chips that convert, the pixels shown, too small / too large refused). Cleaned once: 2004 of our own leftovers (only names our tests make) — Temp from 11.2 GB to 2.5 GB (the rest is other programs'). | ACCEPTED |
