| D193 | **A real crash found on a device: the folder strip's auto-scroll-to-open-folder (D163) can throw during cold-start loading (user, 2026-09-22).** The person's own description matches precisely: on opening the app, the folder strip briefly showed only the "New folder" button (normal — the encrypted database now takes a little longer to open on cold start than before, PBKDF2 key derivation is real work; not itself a bug), then folders appeared, then they started scrolling the strip and the app crashed right after. This is pre-existing code, not touched this session before now: `HomeScreen.kt`'s `LaunchedEffect(state.selectedFolderId, state.folders.size)` (D163) computes `index` from `state.folders` and then suspends (`snapshotFlow { rowState.layoutInfo.viewportSize.width }.first { it > 0 }`) before calling `rowState.scrollToItem(index, -middle)` — if the folder list changes (as it plausibly did here, multiple emissions while the database was still catching up) during that suspension, `index` can be stale by the time it is used, and `LazyListState.scrollToItem` does **not** clamp an out-of-range index — it throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. A concurrent manual scroll (the person's own drag on the same `LazyListState`) is a second, independent way this kind of call can misbehave. **Fixed, defense in depth**: (1) re-check `index` against `rowState.layoutInfo.totalItemsCount`, read fresh right after the suspend point, before using it; (2) wrap the `scrollToItem` call itself in `runCatching` — this is a scroll-position convenience, never something that should be allowed to crash the app, even from a mechanism this fix did not anticipate. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.9.26, compiles, 694 tests pass (no dedicated new test — this is a Compose scroll-state race that Robolectric's synchronous test clock does not reproduce; the fix is a defensive, low-risk change verified by code review, not by reproducing the crash). CONFIRMED by the person 2026-09-22: tested all recent versions on the phone and on BlueStacks, no crash.** |
| D192 | **The database encrypted successfully — D190's fix confirmed! PinPad found broken on a real device: no manual way to submit (user, 2026-09-22).** The person set up a 6-digit PIN; on a later screen the pad only auto-submitted at a known `expectedLength`, and the *first* entry while choosing a brand-new PIN has no fixed length yet by design (D191) — so with no separate confirm button visible/working for them, they kept typing past it, hit `MAX_PIN` (8) with nothing happening, and were locked out of even changing the method. The person's own diagnosis, matching exactly: (1) an Enter button must always be present, not just auto-submit; (2) keep the auto-submit-by-known-length convenience, but submitting a wrong/mismatched PIN should just show "wrong," not silently refuse. **Fixed**: `PinPad` now always shows an **Enter** key in the grid (bottom-left, where an empty placeholder was), enabled whenever there is at least one digit, calling the same `onComplete` auto-submit already uses — so the two paths converge on one outcome instead of Enter being a separate, different mechanism. The PIN-choosing first-entry step in `SecretForm` now routes through this same `onComplete` (length checked there: too short/long shows `lock_error_length`, otherwise advances to the confirm step) instead of a separate external "Continue" `Button` that turned out easy to miss under real use. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.9.25, compiles, 694 tests pass. CONFIRMED by the person 2026-09-22: the PIN pad works correctly now.** |
| D191 | **App lock: a real number-pad for PIN entry, and clickable URLs in note text (user, 2026-09-22).** (1) **PIN entry redesigned**: `PinPad` (`feature/lock/PinPad.kt`) — a 3x4 grid of digit buttons plus backspace, a dot per digit typed, instead of a text field bringing up the system keyboard. Used everywhere a PIN is asked for. When the expected length is already known (`AppLockConfig.pinLength`, new field — the length alone is not a secret, only how many digits it has), the pad submits itself the moment that many digits are typed, no separate button — unlocking, verifying before a change, and the *second* (confirm) entry while setting a new PIN all work this way. The *first* entry while choosing a brand-new PIN cannot auto-submit (its length is what is being chosen), so it keeps an explicit "Continue" button, enabled once the typed length is within `MIN_PIN..MAX_PIN`; a mismatch on confirm restarts both entries with an error, matching the existing pattern/password mismatch UX. Every `SecretForm` caller that already had `config` in scope now threads `config.pinLength` through (`LockScreen`, both `LockSettingsScreen` verify sites, `RecoverySettings.VerifyDialog`); the two setup/creation call sites (`SetupSection`, `ChangeDialog`'s "new secret" step) pass nothing, since `SecretForm` handles the two-phase creation flow internally now. (2) **Clickable links** (closes the long-standing "make Markdown links clickable" gap in `TODO_IDEAS_RU.md`, extended to a case that gap didn't cover): `MarkdownRender`'s inline regex gained a bare-URL alternative (`https?://\S+`) alongside the existing `[text](url)` form — most people paste a raw URL, not markdown syntax. Both forms now render via `AnnotatedString.Builder.appendLink`, using `LinkAnnotation.Url` (Compose's `Text()` opens it via `LocalUriHandler` on tap automatically, no click-handling code needed). This benefits every consumer of `MarkdownRender.inline`/`.preview` at once: text notes' View mode, note-card previews, and — extended to a place `MarkdownRender` was not used before — list note item text and comments (`ListItemCard.kt`, both `ItemCard` and `TotalCard`), which previously had no inline styling or link handling of any kind. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.9.24, compiles, 694 tests pass (no dedicated new tests for either — the PIN pad is pure UI/interaction, and the link regex changes are already covered indirectly by existing `MarkdownRender`-adjacent tests, which still pass; consider a `MarkdownRenderTest` addition later if this area gets touched again). NOT verified on a device: the PIN pad's whole creation/confirm/unlock flow, and that a tapped link genuinely opens the browser (should, given `LinkAnnotation.Url`'s documented default behavior, but this specific app has not exercised it yet).** |
| D190 | **Third attempt at the encryption `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`: the ATTACH target needs the source connection opened with create permission (user, 2026-09-22).** The person's copied error text named the exact failing operation for the first time: `attaching the target database (.../notebudget.db.converting); unable to open database: .../notebudget.db.converting (code 14)` — this is D189's stage-tagging working as intended. SQLCipher's own docs, re-read with this in hand, note (in the context of `PRAGMA cipher_migrate`, but the same underlying constraint) that an operation which creates a database file needs the connection opened with `SQLITE_OPEN_CREATE`, not just `SQLITE_OPEN_READWRITE` — even though the *source* file (the one the connection opens) already exists, `ATTACH` on that connection could not create the brand-new `.converting` file without it. **Fixed**: `DatabaseEncryptor.tryConvert` now opens the source with `CipherDatabase.OPEN_READWRITE or CipherDatabase.CREATE_IF_NECESSARY` (both `openPlain` and `!openPlain` branches); also added a defensive `target.parentFile?.mkdirs()` (harmless — it is the same directory the live file is already in — but cheap insurance). This is the third distinct mechanism tried in this saga (D186 retries, D188 `System.loadLibrary`, now this) — each fixed a real, confirmed problem in sequence (`UnsatisfiedLinkError` really was fixed by D188), so this is not "guess after two wrong guesses," it is "the third real bug in the same code path, found because D189's diagnostics pointed at it precisely." | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.9.24, compiles, 694 tests pass (this remains a real-device-only failure mode; no JVM/Robolectric test can exercise SQLCipher's native ATTACH behavior). NOT yet confirmed on the person's phone — waiting to hear whether the conversion itself now completes.** |
| D189 | **D188's fix was not the whole story: encryption still fails, now with SQLITE_CANTOPEN ("код 14") (user, 2026-09-22).** After D188 (`System.loadLibrary`), the `UnsatisfiedLinkError` is gone — the native library now loads — but the conversion still fails, this time with SQLite's own "Unable to open database file" (error code 14, `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`) somewhere in `/data/user/0/com.notebudget.app/databases/...`. **Two guesses in this saga (D186's transient-lock retries, and initially "`""` vs `null`" for the password) turned out not to be it, or not the whole story** — rather than guess a third time, this entry only adds precision: `DatabaseEncryptor.tryConvert` now tags a `stage` string (opening the source / checkpointing the WAL / attaching the target / running `sqlcipher_export` / detaching / reopening to verify / swapping the files) and includes it in the failure message, so the next report names the exact operation instead of a bare SQLite error code. Also added: a **"Copy error text"** button next to the diagnostic line in Settings — the person has been retyping English/technical exception text phonetically into Russian by hand, which is lossy (this round's report needed real reverse-engineering — unzipping the built APK and the `sqlcipher-android` AAR — to even get back to the actual `UnsatisfiedLinkError` wording that led to D188); copy-paste removes that entirely for whatever comes back next. 2 new strings x 10 languages. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: diagnostics-only, DONE in 0.9.23, compiles, 694 tests pass. Deliberately no fix attempted this round** — retries already exist (D186) and did not resolve this on the person's phone, so guessing a third mechanism without more information was judged worse than asking for the precise, copied stage+message first. Waiting on that before the next attempt.** |
| D188 | **The real cause of D186's "Could not encrypt the database" found and fixed: the SQLCipher native library was never loaded (user, 2026-09-22, after installing D187's fix — the app itself stopped crash-looping, confirmed, but encryption still failed).** D186 only masked the symptom (a generic message) with retries and a shown-but-unexplained diagnostic; this time the diagnostic text D186 added did its job and showed the real exception: `UnsatisfiedLinkError: no implementation found for long net.zetetic.database.sqlcipher.SQLiteConnection.native...`. Investigated without a device: unzipped the built APK and the `sqlcipher-android` AAR directly — `libsqlcipher.so` is genuinely present for all four ABIs (arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, x86, x86_64), correctly packaged, not a missing/wrong-architecture file; the library exports `JNI_OnLoad` and internally references the right `net/zetetic/database/sqlcipher/*` classes, so the `.so` itself is not the problem. The real cause: `net.zetetic:sqlcipher-android`, unlike most Android native libraries, does **not** load itself automatically — its own README shows every single usage example starting with an explicit `System.loadLibrary("sqlcipher")` call before any database operation. This project never made that call anywhere, so the native methods were simply never wired up. **Fixed**: `core/database/SqlCipherNativeLibrary.kt` (`ensureLoaded()`, safe to call repeatedly — the JVM tracks what is already loaded), called at the top of all three places that touch SQLCipher: `DatabaseEncryptor.tryConvert`, `RawDatabaseTransfer.import`, and `AppDatabase.create` (before building `SupportOpenHelperFactory`). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.9.22, compiles, 694 tests pass (this bug is not something a JVM/Robolectric test can catch — it is purely about the real native `.so` never being loaded on a real device — so there is deliberately no new automated test for it; the existing `DatabaseEncryptionStoreTest` from D187 is unrelated). NOT yet confirmed on the person's actual phone — waiting to hear whether encryption itself now works, not just whether the app stays up.** |
| D187 | **The real bug behind D186 found and fixed: a crash loop after a failed encryption attempt (user, 2026-09-22).** The person's phone crashed on every launch after the encryption attempt in D186 failed and the app was closed. Root cause, found this time (D186 was only a mitigation without a device to reproduce on): `DatabaseEncryptionStore.readBlocking()` returned the stored `passphrase` **unconditionally**, while its `enabled` flag was correctly gated — so after `generatePassphrase()` saves a passphrase (always, before the conversion even runs) and the conversion then fails (`setEnabled(true)` is only called on success, per `SettingsScreen.toggle`), the leftover passphrase was still handed to `AppDatabase.create(context, passphrase)` in `AppContainer`, which then tried to open the **still-plaintext** database file through SQLCipher with that stale passphrase — failing on every single launch from then on, an unrecoverable crash loop without reinstalling. **Fixed at the single source**: `readBlocking()` now returns `passphrase = null` whenever the raw `enabled` flag is `false`, regardless of what happens to be sealed in storage — matches how `enabled` itself was already computed, so the two fields can no longer disagree. **The person's actual notes and folders were never at risk**: `DatabaseEncryptor` only swaps the live file for the converted one after the new file is confirmed to open (§"Database encryption" design, D184) — since the conversion failed, that swap never happened, the original plaintext file was untouched throughout. New test `DatabaseEncryptionStoreTest` (Robolectric, `PlainSecretBox`) pins this exact scenario (a passphrase generated for a failed attempt must read back as `null`) so it cannot regress silently again. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: DONE in 0.9.21, code fix + regression test, compiles, tests pass (694). Recovery for the person's already-crashing 0.9.20 install: installing 0.9.21 over it (no uninstall, no data wipe) should be enough — the fix only changes what the app reads on startup, and the underlying database file was never touched by the failed attempt. NOT independently re-verified on the person's actual phone yet — this is what needs to be confirmed next.** |
| D186 | **Database-encryption failure on a real phone, mitigated (user, 2026-09-22).** The person turned on Settings -> Data -> Database encryption on a real device and got the generic "Could not encrypt the database" message (D184's conversion). No Android device/emulator exists in this development environment to reproduce or attach a debugger, so the exact cause could not be confirmed; the most likely one, given `container.database.close()` runs immediately before `DatabaseEncryptor` opens a second connection to the same file, is the OS not releasing the first connection's lock synchronously with `close()` on some devices. Two changes, not exclusive: (1) `DatabaseEncryptor.convert` now retries the whole attempt up to 3 times, 300 ms apart, before giving up (`ATTEMPTS`, `RETRY_DELAY_MS`), which rides out exactly that kind of transient lock; (2) the real failure message (`DatabaseEncryptionResult.Failed.message`) is now shown in the Settings UI itself (`failDetail`, small red text under the generic message) and logged (`Log.e("DatabaseEncryption", ...)`), instead of being silently discarded — so if it fails again, the actual reason is visible without another guess-and-ship round trip. Also checked and ruled out during this: passing `""` vs `null` as the SQLCipher password to open a plaintext database behaves identically in `net.zetetic:sqlcipher-android` (`password != null && password.length > 0` gates whether a key is applied at all), so that was never the bug. | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: code DONE, compiles, 687+ tests pass. Still NOT verified on a device — this is a mitigation and a diagnostic improvement, not a confirmed fix; if it fails again, `failDetail`'s text is the next real lead.** |
| D185 | **App lock: PIN code, vertical method list (bug fix), and a configurable wrong-try policy including an opt-in wipe (user, 2026-09-22).** (1) **Bug fixed:** the method chooser in the "Change password/method" dialog (`ChangeDialog`) used a plain `Row`, which does not wrap — inside the dialog's fixed width the third chip (Login and password) was pushed off screen and unreachable. The original Settings -> App lock setup screen already used `FlowRow` and did not have this bug. (2) **Both places now use a vertical list, one method per line** (the person's own request, not just a fix): `FilterChip` per method, full width, in a `Column`, `LOCK_METHODS`. (3) **A fourth method, PIN code** (`LockMethod.PIN`): digits only, 4-8 long (`AppLockUseCases.MIN_PIN`/`MAX_PIN`), numeric keyboard (`SecretForm`, `KeyboardType.NumberPassword`), hashed exactly like a password (no new storage format). **New order everywhere a method is chosen: PIN, Pattern, Password, Login and password** (was Password, Pattern, Login and password) — the default choice on first setup is now PIN too. (4) **The wrong-try wait is now configurable, not fixed** (`AppLockConfig.freeTries` default 5 unchanged, `AppLockConfig.baseWaitSeconds` default **10 s**, was a hardcoded 30 s; still doubles, still capped at 15 minutes — `AppLockUseCases.waitAfter(attempts, freeTries, baseWaitSeconds)`, `updateLockoutPolicy`). (5) **New: a total-attempts limit, off by default** (`AppLockConfig.maxAttempts = 0`): once reached, `AppLockConfig.lockoutMode` decides what happens — **`RECOVERY_ONLY`** (default when the limit is turned on): direct guessing is refused entirely (`AppLockConfig.lockedOut`, `UnlockResult.LockedOut`), only a recovery method (question / e-mail / authenticator) can get back in, exactly as it already could; or **`WIPE`** (opt-in, the person's own explicit second request after being asked "recovery-only, or do you really want irreversible wipe" — they said support both): the app's own data is erased (`core/security/AppDataWiper.kt`, `AndroidAppDataWiper`: deletes `filesDir`, `cacheDir`, `databases`, `shared_prefs`, `noBackupFilesDir`, then kills the process — plain file deletion, not `ActivityManager.clearApplicationUserData()`, which is not reliably callable by an app on itself). `WIPE` cannot be turned on with a tap: `WipeConfirmDialog` requires typing a confirmation phrase first (§1.3 dangerous-action rule — states exactly what is erased, that it is instant and irreversible). A `null` wiper (e.g. a test build) fails safe into `RECOVERY_ONLY` behavior rather than guessing. `UnlockResult` gained `LockedOut` and `Wiped`; every place in the app that matches on `UnlockResult` was checked and updated (`LockScreen`, `LockSettingsScreen` x2, `RecoverySettings`, `RecoveryUi` x3) — three of those (`RecoveryUi.kt`) used `else -> onDone()` as a catch-all that would otherwise have treated a lockout or a wipe as a **successful** recovery, which would have been a real bug. 21 new strings x 10 languages. Tests: `AppLockUseCasesTest` (configurable wait, total-limit lockout with a fake wiper recording the call, the no-wiper fail-safe path, PIN validation) — the wait-cap test's expected values changed (10 s/20 s/40 s base, not 30/60/120) to match the new default, and the steps cap inside `waitAfter` was raised from 5 to 20 so the 15-minute cap still actually triggers with a 10 s base (at base=30s/steps=5 the old numbers happened to already exceed the cap; at the new base=10s they no longer did without this fix — found by the updated test failing, not by inspection). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: code DONE, compiles, unit-tested (including the wipe trigger, via a fake wiper — the real device deletion path itself is not exercised by a test on purpose). NOT verified on a device: the actual UI (vertical list, PIN keyboard), and especially the wipe confirmation flow end to end — treat this exactly as carefully on a device as D184's encryption, it is equally hard to undo a mistake in.** |
| D184 | **Database encryption, opt-in in Settings (user, 2026-09-22).** Settings -> Data -> **Database encryption**: a switch, off by default (existing installs are unaffected until the person turns it on themselves — asked and confirmed, not automatic on update). Turning it on converts the live database file **in place** through SQLCipher (`net.zetetic:sqlcipher-android:4.19.0`, `sqlcipher_export()`: attach a new file with the key, copy every table, verify the copy opens, only then swap the file — a failure midway leaves the original untouched, `core/database/DatabaseEncryptor.kt`); no data is lost either direction, on or off. The passphrase is **not typed by the person** — the app generates 256 random bits (base64), seals it with the phone's Keystore the same way as the backup password (D134, `core/security/DatabaseEncryptionStore.kt`, `SecretBox`), and shows it behind Show / Hide with a **Copy** button so it can be written down or copied elsewhere. Because `AppContainer.database` is a process-scoped `by lazy` (not Activity-scoped), the app needs a full **process restart** after either direction, not just `Activity.recreate()` (`DatabaseEncryptionOptions`, `restartAppProcess`); the person is told and offered a "Restart now" button. `AppDatabase.create(context, passphrase)`: `passphrase == null` opens exactly as before (unchanged for every install that never turns this on); `passphrase != null` opens through `net.zetetic.database.sqlcipher.SupportOpenHelperFactory` — same entities, same `MIGRATIONS`, same schema, only what opens the file differs. **Compatibility, answered for the user:** an encrypted database file cannot be opened by an older app build or by a plain SQLite tool — but this only affects the raw file; the existing ZIP + JSON backup (§6.13) is untouched by this decision and stays the one format guaranteed to open on any version, because it goes through the app's own domain objects, never the database file itself. So: back up on the old build, install the new one, restore the same backup — everything comes back, encrypted or not. **A second, additional way to move data** (the person specifically asked for this, next to the JSON backup): Settings -> Data -> Database encryption also has **Export database file** / **Import database file** (`core/database/RawDatabaseTransfer.kt`): the raw file, whatever it currently is (checkpointed with `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(FULL)` first so the exported file is self-contained); import detects plaintext vs. encrypted from the file header, asks for the password only when needed, opens a read-only check against it (and confirms a `note` table exists) before touching anything, and only then replaces the live file — behind a destructive-action confirmation dialog (§1.3) because, unlike the JSON import, there is no merge mode here: it replaces everything. New dependency: `androidx.sqlite:sqlite:2.7.0` (pinned per the library's own README, alongside `sqlcipher-android`). **Recovery by e-mail (added same day, the person's own request):** next to Copy, a **"Send password by e-mail"** button reuses the app-lock recovery mail's already-configured SMTP settings (D129, `AppLockUseCases.smtpSettings()` / `sendTestMail`, no separate mail setup) to send the plaintext passphrase to the person's own configured recovery address, behind a confirmation dialog that says exactly what this means (readable by anyone with access to that mailbox) — this is a deliberate, explicit choice to trade some security for "I will not lose access to my own data," the same trade-off D129 already made for the app-lock recovery code, not a new category of risk for this app. Disabled with a clear message when e-mail recovery is not configured yet. 7 more strings x 10 languages (34 total for this decision). | ACCEPTED. **STATUS: code DONE, compiles; JVM/Robolectric tests cannot exercise the real SQLCipher native path on this development machine (no Android device/emulator here — Robolectric runs on the host JVM, SQLCipher's native library is built for Android ABIs only), so the conversion, the raw export/import and the restart flow are all **NOT verified on a device** — add to the next build's device checklist (§21.4b): turn encryption on, confirm the password shows/copies, restart, confirm the data is intact; turn it back off; export the file, wipe the app, import it back; try a wrong password on import.** |

**Notes on the log (added in agent-1.1):**

- Several rows repeat the same decision under different IDs. IDs are kept
  because other text refers to them; the duplicate groups are: D16 = D45 = D75
  (no cloud sync), D17 = D51 = D72 (flat folders), D18 = D71 (one folder per
  note), D19 = D49 = D73 (last folder cannot be deleted), D20 = D48 = D74
  (delete or move notes when deleting a folder), D22 = D46 (full export/import).
  New decisions continue from **D196**.
- "ACCEPTED" means the product decision is accepted. It does **not** mean it is
  implemented — implementation status lives in §21.
- D27 / D64 (e-mail recovery) conflict with D16 / D75 (no server) — see gap G02
  in §18.1.

---

## 5. Key invariants

The agent must satisfy the following invariants.

### INV-1

A note always has a `folderId`.

```text
Note.folderId != null
```

### INV-2

The app always has at least one folder.

```text
count(Folder) >= 1
```

### INV-3

A folder's featured note must belong to that folder.

```text
folder.featuredNoteId == null || featuredNote.folderId == folder.id
```

### INV-4

Non-leaf budget nodes do not store user sum/goal as the source of truth. Their
values are computed from children.

### INV-5

The remainder is always computed as:

```text
Remainder = Goal − Sum
```

### INV-6

Any entity deletion is possible only after user confirmation.

### INV-7

After deleting a folder, notes must not become orphans.

### INV-8

A one-time reminder must survive device reboots and be restored.

### INV-9

Passwords, pattern locks, and other secrets are not stored in plaintext.

### INV-10

If app lock is enabled, note data must not be accessible in the UI before a
successful unlock.

### INV-11

Export does not reveal secrets (passwords, hashes, biometrics).

### INV-12

Import does not violate INV-1: when importing notes, a current/chosen folder is
assigned automatically.

### INV-13

After import, all cascading recalculation (budget, featured note) is performed
again.

---

## 6. Functional requirements

### 6.1 App lock

Supported methods:

1. Password.
2. 3×3 pattern.
3. Login + password (local account).
4. Biometrics (via system `BiometricPrompt`).

Requirements:

- Lock settings live in the security section.
- Lock can be enabled or disabled.
- If lock is enabled, an unlock screen is shown on launch.
- After successful unlock, the main screen opens.
- Biometrics must not be the only way in: a fallback method is required.
- Password and login/password use salted hashing (PBKDF2 or Argon2).
- Secrets are never logged.
- Biometric data is not stored by the app.

Behavior:

1. App launch.
2. If lock is enabled — show lock screen.
3. Verify.
4. On success — main screen; on failure — error and retry.

Roles (D31, D66): local accounts. Admin has full access, user is read-only; the
admin grants which notes each user can read.

Lock timeout (D30): configurable in seconds/minutes, can be disabled; value `0`
means lock immediately on background.

Recovery (D27, D28, D64): security questions and optionally e-mail. E-mail
reset requires a server/network layer, which contradicts D16 / D75 (no server).
The mechanism is **not defined** — see gap G02 in §18.1. Until it is resolved,
only security-question recovery may be implemented.

### 6.2 First launch and first folder

- You cannot create a note until at least one folder exists.
- The first screen lets the user set: name, color, style, optionally icon, and
  shows a preview.
- The first folder is an ordinary user folder and is fully editable.
- If the folder is the only one — deletion is forbidden.
- Default name is "My Notes" (D21).

### 6.3 Folders

Folder properties:

- unique ID;
- name (unique, D52);
- color;
- style (`FolderStyleId`);
- optional icon or emoji;
- sort order;
- creation and update dates;
- optional featured note;
- featured note display settings.

Rules:

- a folder is only for organizing notes;
- a folder never participates in budget calculations;
- folders are flat (D17, D51, D72);
- a note belongs to exactly one folder (D18, D71);
- deleting a folder requires confirmation;
- the last folder cannot be deleted (D19, D49, D73);
- when deleting a non-last folder, the user chooses: move notes to another
  folder or delete together with the folder (D20, D48, D74);
- folder sorting: manual drag + auto (date, name, color, index/UUID) (D54).

Folder card may display:

- name;
- color and style;
- icon or monogram;
- note count;
- featured note;
- selected featured metrics;
- optional progress (D35, D67).

### 6.4 Folder featured note

A folder can have one featured note (D07). Any note in the folder can be the
featured one, not only a budget note (D40). If the featured note is a budget
note, the folder card can show sum, goal, remainder, or any combination (D08).

Values are taken from the root node of the featured budget note:

```text
Folder sum       = root sum of the featured budget note
Folder goal      = root goal of the featured budget note
Folder remainder = root remainder of the featured budget note
```

Settings:

- which note is featured;
- show sum / goal / remainder;
- compact (`COMPACT`) or full (`FULL`) mode.

Behavior:

- values update automatically;
- when the featured note is deleted, the link is cleared;
- when the note is moved to another folder, the link is cleared;
- if the note is not a budget note — sum/goal/remainder display is unavailable.

A folder is **not** an automatic sum of all its notes. Auto-sum of a folder's
budget notes is a separate feature (D41).

### 6.5 Notes

Types: TEXT, DRAWING, LIST, BUDGET, AUDIO. The type is an open id (D82): a note of a kind
this version does not know is kept intact and shown with a placeholder.

Required properties: id, folderId, title, type, colorHex (nullable), isPinned,
isFavorite, isTitleBold, contentText (nullable), createdAt, updatedAt, deletedAt
(nullable, D81), extras (nullable JSON with type-specific settings, D86).

Rules:

- the title is required (D55);
- default color is transparent — inherits the folder color (D56);
- pin (`isPinned`) and favorite (`isFavorite`) are different properties;
- `isTitleBold` — bold display of the title;
- sorting: pinned → favorite → updatedAt, with optional manual and auto
  sorting (D57);
- moving between folders: if the note was featured — the link is cleared;
  reminders are preserved;
- deletion (D29, D81): only with confirmation; the note goes to the trash (`deletedAt`
  is set), its alarms are cancelled, `featuredNoteId` is cleared; reminders and budget
  nodes are kept until the trash is emptied (Phase 10).

### 6.6 Budget note

A recursive node tree (`BudgetNode`).

**Leaf** — sum and goal are entered manually, remainder is computed.

**Group** — sum and goal are computed from children, remainder is computed.

Formulas:

```text
Remainder = Goal − Sum

Group sum       = Σ child sums
Group goal      = Σ child goals
Group remainder = Group goal − Group sum
```

A LEAF has a **quantity** (D94, 1..9999, default 1); the sum and goal typed on a leaf are for one piece:

```text
Leaf sum  = manual sum × quantity
Leaf goal = manual goal × quantity
```

Cascading recalculation runs on any change: editing a leaf's sum/goal,
adding/removing/moving a node — recalculate upward to the root.

A negative remainder is allowed; no separate "goal type" flag is needed.

Visual representation:

- a group is a card: header on top, children inside, totals row on the bottom;
- any group can be collapsed; collapsed view shows name + aggregates.

Additionally: a comment on any node (D34, D70); an operation log per item
(D36); budget periods are configurable (D33, D68).

### 6.7 List note

List items: text, optional comment (D70), quantity and unit of measure (D92).

Units (D37, D69, D92) are an app setting. Built-in units, which cannot be deleted: pieces, kilograms, litres, metres and the currencies rouble, US dollar, pound sterling, euro. The user can add custom units and delete custom ones only.

A list can hold **unit totals** ("Total by unit"): a row with a name, a comment and a unit whose result is the sum of the quantities of all items of the list in that unit. Several totals with different units can live in one list. Storage and rules: D96.

### 6.8 Text note

Format — Markdown (D38): headings, lists, emphasis, checklists `- [x]` and
`- [ ]`. Rich text is not required.

### 6.9 Drawing note

Raster image (D39, D83): one PNG per note, fixed 1080x1440 sheet. Tools: pencil, brush,
eraser; colour and width; undo. Export to PNG/JPEG (D42). A vector layer and bucket fill
are in progress and not yet specified (§21.3).
Since 0.9.65 the sheet has its own size (D260); since 0.9.70 its own background — a colour and a degree of transparency, under both
layers, part of every exported / shared picture (D265).

### 6.9.1 Audio note (D88)

One `.m4a` clip per note (AAC, mono, 44.1 kHz, 64 kbit/s), recorded with `MediaRecorder`,
played with `MediaPlayer`. The `RECORD_AUDIO` permission is asked on the first tap on
Record. A recording is finished and kept when the app goes to the background; recording
again replaces the clip only if the new recording is valid.

### 6.10 Reminders and notifications

Base type — `ONE_TIME`, but repeats are supported (D59) and multiple reminders
per note are supported (D58).

Requirements:

- fires while the app is closed;
- restored after a reboot (INV-8);
- reacts correctly to time and timezone changes;
- can be cancelled and edited;
- status updates after firing.

Technical recommendations:

- `AlarmManager` with `setExactAndAllowWhileIdle()` or `setAlarmClock()`;
- `BroadcastReceiver` for firing;
- `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED` for restore;
- respect Android exact-alarm restrictions (`canScheduleExactAlarms()`);
- `NotificationManager` + channels;
- `FullScreenIntent` for pop-up notifications if the platform allows.

Reminder parameters: date/time, sound, vibration, popup notification, requires
confirmation, repeat until confirmed.

If "requires confirmation" is on — until confirmed, the reminder repeats at a
configurable interval (this is a repeat of the same reminder, not a periodic
reminder).

Statuses: `SCHEDULED`, `FIRED`, `AWAITING_CONFIRMATION`, `COMPLETED`,
`CANCELLED`.

Privacy (D60): notification content is configurable — full text / title only /
generic text.

Since 0.9.71: a reminder still to ring can be switched off and on (`isEnabled`, D268); Cancel asks first (D269); a tap on a
reminder notification opens the calendar on its day with the reminder framed (D267); the quiet status notification also counts
the tasks (D266).

### 6.11 Localization

Languages: Russian, English, French, German (D15).

- all strings via string resources;
- default language from system settings;
- manual language choice is persisted;
- help and glossary are localized;
- numbers, dates, and currencies are formatted per locale.

### 6.12 Themes and customization

- light and dark themes;
- color schemes;
- styles for buttons, notes, folders;
- UI density.

Folder style respects light/dark theme. Color must not be the only way to
convey meaning.

### 6.13 Export and import

**Full backup**: one file with all data (folders, notes, nodes, reminders,
settings) **without secrets** (INV-11). Format — ZIP + JSON inside (stated as
accepted in the original document; it has no separate Decision Log entry —
`D41`/`Q41` cited earlier was a dangling reference, D41 is about folder
auto-sum); file name includes date/time, e.g.
`notebudget-backup-2026-09-18.json.zip`. Saved via SAF (D26). Before import, a
dialog warns about overwrite; conflict behavior — merge / replace / new UUIDs
(D63). This format goes through the app's own domain objects, never the raw
database file, so it stays the one export guaranteed to open on any app
version regardless of whether the database is encrypted (D184).

**Database file** (D184, Settings -> Data -> Database encryption): a second,
lower-level export/import, next to the one above — the raw `.db` file, whatever
it currently is (plain or SQLCipher-encrypted). Unlike the full backup, import
here has no merge mode: it replaces the live file outright, behind the
dangerous-action confirmation of §1.3.

**Individual notes** (D23, D62):

- TEXT → `.md`;
- DRAWING → `.png` (+ optional JSON with strokes);
- LIST → JSON with structure OR text snapshot;
- BUDGET → JSON with node tree OR text snapshot.

For LIST and BUDGET two modes are supported (D24):

1. **With structure** — tree/items are saved, restores to the original type.
2. **Result only** — text snapshot with totals; restores as a TEXT note.

Note import: the user picks a file, the app determines the type by
extension/signature, assigns the current/chosen folder (INV-12), and shows an
error on unrecognized type.

Invariants: INV-11, INV-12, INV-13.

### 6.14 Trash (D29, D65, D81, D93)

Deleting a note or folder moves it to the trash (soft delete). The Trash screen shows what is there, newest first, with the deletion date and the days left before automatic removal. Per item: **Restore** and **Delete forever**; for the whole trash: **Empty trash**. Every permanent deletion is confirmed and irreversible. Retention is a setting (7, 14, 30 default, 60, 90 days, or never). See D93 for the rules of restoring a note whose folder is in the trash.

---

## 7. Data model

### 7.1 Common conventions

- ID: `String` (UUID).
- Time: UTC epoch millis (`Long`).
- Money: `Long` in minor units.
- Flags: `Boolean`.
- Enums: `String` or `Int` with explicit mapping.
- Deleting a note cascade-deletes related data.
- Deleting a folder never leaves notes without a folder.

### 7.2 Enums

```kotlin
// D82: an open id, not an enum, so a new kind is one registry entry
@JvmInline value class NoteType(val id: String) {
    companion object {
        val TEXT = NoteType("TEXT"); val DRAWING = NoteType("DRAWING")
        val LIST = NoteType("LIST"); val BUDGET = NoteType("BUDGET")
        val AUDIO = NoteType("AUDIO")
    }
}

enum class FolderStyleId {
    MINIMAL, SOFT, GRADIENT, PATTERN, OUTLINE, COMPACT
}

enum class FeaturedMetric { SUM, GOAL, REMAINDER }

enum class FeaturedLayout { TITLE_ONLY, COMPACT, FULL }

enum class BudgetNodeKind { LEAF, GROUP }

enum class ReminderType { ONE_TIME }

enum class ReminderStatus {
    SCHEDULED, FIRED, AWAITING_CONFIRMATION, COMPLETED, CANCELLED
}

enum class LockMethod {
    NONE, PASSWORD, PATTERN_3X3, LOGIN_PASSWORD, BIOMETRIC
}
```

### 7.3 Folder

```kotlin
data class Folder(
    val id: String,
    val name: String,
    val colorHex: String,
    val styleId: String,
    val icon: String?,
    val sortOrder: Int,

    val featuredNoteId: String?,
    val featuredMetricsMask: Int,
    val featuredLayout: FeaturedLayout,

    val createdAt: Long,
    val updatedAt: Long,
    val deletedAt: Long? = null      // trash, D81
)
```

Metrics mask: `SUM = 1`, `GOAL = 2`, `REMAINDER = 4`. Example: `7` — all three.

`styleId` stores `FolderStyleId.name`. `FeaturedLayout` has three values in the
enum (`TITLE_ONLY`, `COMPACT`, `FULL`) while §6.4 lists only `COMPACT` / `FULL`
— see gap G05.

### 7.4 Note

```kotlin
data class Note(
    val id: String,
    val folderId: String,

    val title: String,
    val type: NoteType,
    val colorHex: String?,

    val isPinned: Boolean,
    val isFavorite: Boolean,
    val isTitleBold: Boolean,

    val contentText: String?,

    val createdAt: Long,
    val updatedAt: Long,
    val deletedAt: Long? = null,     // trash, D81
    val extras: String? = null       // type-specific JSON, D86 (e.g. BudgetLayout, D87)
)
```

`folderId` must not be `null`.

### 7.5 BudgetNode

```kotlin
data class BudgetNode(
    val id: String,
    val noteId: String,
    val parentId: String?,

    val title: String,
    val kind: BudgetNodeKind,

    val manualSumMinor: Long,
    val manualGoalMinor: Long,

    val comment: String?,

    val cachedSumMinor: Long,
    val cachedGoalMinor: Long,
    val cachedRemainderMinor: Long,

    val isExpanded: Boolean,
    val sortOrder: Int,

    val createdAt: Long,
    val updatedAt: Long,
    val quantity: Int = 1     // D94: 1..9999, LEAF only
)
```

Rules:

- a budget note has one root node with `parentId == null`;
- for `LEAF` the source is `manual*` (for ONE piece) times `quantity` (D94);
- for `GROUP` the source is computed values;
- `cachedRemainderMinor = cachedGoalMinor - cachedSumMinor`.

### 7.6 Reminder

```kotlin
data class Reminder(
    val id: String,
    val noteId: String,

    val type: ReminderType,
    val triggerAt: Long,

    val isEnabled: Boolean,
    val status: ReminderStatus,

    val soundEnabled: Boolean,
    val soundUri: String?,
    val vibrationEnabled: Boolean,
    val popupEnabled: Boolean,

    val requiresConfirmation: Boolean,
    val repeatUntilConfirmed: Boolean,
    val repeatIntervalMinutes: Int?,

    val createdAt: Long,
    val updatedAt: Long
)
```

### 7.7 AppLockSettings

Stored in secure storage (EncryptedSharedPreferences or Android Keystore), not
in the ordinary DB.

```kotlin
data class AppLockSettings(
    val enabled: Boolean,
    val primaryMethod: LockMethod,
    val biometricEnabled: Boolean,
    val fallbackMethod: LockMethod?,

    val username: String?,
    val credentialSalt: String?,
    val credentialHash: String?,
    val patternHash: String?,

    val lockTimeoutSeconds: Int?,
    val lastUnlockAt: Long?
)
```

Requirements: salt + strong KDF, Keystore, no secrets in logs.

---

## 8. DB schema (Room / SQLite)

```sql
CREATE TABLE folder (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
    name TEXT NOT NULL,
    color_hex TEXT NOT NULL,
    style_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    icon TEXT,
    sort_order INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,

    featured_note_id TEXT,
    featured_metrics_mask INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    featured_layout TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'COMPACT',

    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    deleted_at INTEGER                      -- trash, D81
);

CREATE TABLE note (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
    folder_id TEXT NOT NULL,

    title TEXT NOT NULL,
    type TEXT NOT NULL,
    color_hex TEXT,

    is_pinned INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    is_favorite INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    is_title_bold INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,

    content_text TEXT,

    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    deleted_at INTEGER,                     -- trash, D81
    extras TEXT,                            -- schema v2 (D86), type-specific JSON

    FOREIGN KEY (folder_id)
        REFERENCES folder(id)
        ON DELETE RESTRICT
);

CREATE TABLE budget_node (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
    note_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    parent_id TEXT,

    title TEXT NOT NULL,
    kind TEXT NOT NULL,

    manual_sum_minor INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    manual_goal_minor INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,

    comment TEXT,

    cached_sum_minor INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    cached_goal_minor INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    cached_remainder_minor INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,

    is_expanded INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    sort_order INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,

    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    quantity INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,    -- schema v3 (D94)

    FOREIGN KEY (note_id)
        REFERENCES note(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE,

    FOREIGN KEY (parent_id)
        REFERENCES budget_node(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE
);

CREATE TABLE reminder (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
    note_id TEXT NOT NULL,

    type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ONE_TIME',
    trigger_at INTEGER NOT NULL,

    is_enabled INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'SCHEDULED',

    sound_enabled INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    sound_uri TEXT,
    vibration_enabled INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    popup_enabled INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,

    requires_confirmation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    repeat_until_confirmed INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    repeat_interval_minutes INTEGER,

    created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
    updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL,

    FOREIGN KEY (note_id)
        REFERENCES note(id)
        ON DELETE CASCADE
);
```

> **Current schema is version 3** (`app/schemas/.../3.json`; v1 -> v2 = `note.extras` (D86),
> v2 -> v3 = `budget_node.quantity` (D94); each has a migration + `MigrationTest`). Drawings and audio live in files
> (`filesDir/drawings`, `filesDir/audio`), not in the DB (D83, D88).
>
> **Still incomplete.** Tables/fields for tags, list items, units, budget operation log,
> budget periods, currency, repeating reminders, and local user accounts are **not
> designed yet** — see gap G01 in §18.1. Each is designed at the start of the phase that
> needs it, with a Room migration.
>
> `note.title` is `NOT NULL` without a default; a non-blank title is validated in
> the use case (D55).

Integrity of `folder.featured_note_id → note.id`:

- store `featured_note_id` on the folder;
- verify note belongs to the folder in repository/use case;
- on note delete or move — clear `featured_note_id`;
- use transactions.

---

## 9. Architecture

### 9.1 Stack

- Kotlin, Coroutines, Flow
- Jetpack Compose, Material 3, Navigation Compose
- MVVM + UDF
- Room
- DataStore
- Hilt (or manual DI at the start)
- AlarmManager, BroadcastReceiver, NotificationManagerCompat
- BiometricPrompt, Android Keystore
- kotlinx.serialization for local structures

### 9.2 Packages

```text
com.notebudget.app
├── core
│   ├── common
│   ├── database
│   ├── datastore
│   ├── security
│   ├── notification
│   ├── alarm
│   ├── ui
│   └── util
├── domain
│   ├── model
│   ├── repository
│   └── usecase
├── data
│   ├── local            (Room lives in core/database)
│   ├── repository
│   ├── mapper
│   ├── drawing          (per-note file stores: png / m4a / json)
│   └── transfer         (backup + single-note export/import, D84, D85)
└── feature
    ├── lock             (not created yet)
    ├── onboarding
    ├── home
    ├── folders
    ├── notes            (+ notes/types registry, D82)
    ├── budget
    ├── drawing
    ├── audio
    ├── reminders        (not created yet)
    ├── settings
    └── help             (backup UI lives in settings)
```

### 9.3 Layer rules

- UI never talks to Room directly.
- ViewModel contains no SQL.
- Repository is responsible for the data source.
- UseCase holds business logic when it is complex.
- Domain models are independent of Android frameworks where possible.
- All data changes go through repository/use case.
- Complex operations run in transactions.

---

## 10. Android specifics

### 10.1 Permissions

```text
POST_NOTIFICATIONS
SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM
USE_EXACT_ALARM
RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED
USE_BIOMETRIC
VIBRATE
WAKE_LOCK
USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT
RECORD_AUDIO          (audio notes, D88; asked at runtime on first Record)
```

The exact set depends on final reminder behavior and target SDK. **Declared in the
manifest today:** `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`, `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`, `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`,
`VIBRATE`, `RECORD_AUDIO`, `USE_BIOMETRIC`. Not declared: `USE_EXACT_ALARM`,
`USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT`, `WAKE_LOCK` (G08).

### 10.2 Exact alarms

- check `canScheduleExactAlarms()`;
- show a clear explanation if the permission is missing;
- do not rely on WorkManager alone.

### 10.3 Reboot

`BOOT_COMPLETED` receiver: reads active reminders, drops overdue ones per
rules, reschedules future ones.

### 10.4 Time and timezone

Handle `ACTION_TIME_CHANGED` and `ACTION_TIMEZONE_CHANGED`; reschedule
reminders.

### 10.5 Notifications

- notification channels;
- sound, vibration, popup;
- do not show sensitive content while the app is locked;
- actions: open note, complete, snooze (if confirmed).

### 10.6 minSdk / targetSdk

`minSdk = 28`, `targetSdk = 36` (D61).

---

## 11. Main screens

1. **Splash / init** — check folders, lock state, restore reminders.
2. **Lock screen** — password / pattern / login+password / biometrics, error,
   route to recovery.
3. **First-folder onboarding** — name, color, style, icon, preview.
4. **Main screen** — combined: folders and notes together (D50).
5. **Folder screen** — header, style, notes, create, sort, search, settings.
6. **Note screen** — per type: text, drawing, list, budget; common elements:
   title, color, folder, favorite, pin, bold title, reminders, actions menu.
7. **Budget note screen** — tree, groups, leaves, sums, goals, remainder, root
   total.
8. **Folder settings** — name, color, style, icon, featured note, metrics,
   layout, delete.
9. **Security settings** — enable/disable, method, change password/pattern,
   biometrics, timeout, notification privacy.
10. **App settings** — language, theme, sound, vibration, popups, currency,
    trash retention, help, export/import.
11. **Trash** — deleted folders and notes, restore, delete forever, empty trash.

---

## 12. UI requirements

### 12.1 Note card

Title, type, content preview, color, folder, favorite star, pin, reminder
indicator, updated date. Bold title if `isTitleBold`.

### 12.2 Folder card

Name, color, style, icon/monogram, note count, featured note, selected metrics.
Example:

```text
Family finances
12 notes

Family budget
Sum: 200,000
Goal: 1,000,000
Remainder: 800,000
```

### 12.3 Pinned and favorite

Recommended sort: pinned → favorite → the rest; within groups by update date or
manually.

### 12.4 Accessibility

- text contrast per WCAG;
- color is not the only meaning carrier;
- `content description` for icons;
- TalkBack support;
- system font size support;
- touch target ≥ 48dp;
- colors adapt in dark theme.

---

## 13. Security

- Password is not stored in plaintext.
- Pattern lock is not stored in plaintext.
- Salt + strong KDF.
- Android Keystore for protecting keys.
- Secrets are never logged or sent to crash reports.
- Biometrics only via system `BiometricPrompt`.
- Fallback method is required.
- Notifications do not reveal content while the app is locked.
- Database encryption is opt-in, off by default (D184): a Settings switch
  converts the live SQLite file to SQLCipher in place; the passphrase is
  app-generated (never typed), sealed with the Keystore the same way as the
  backup password (D134), and shown behind Show / Hide with Copy.

---

## 14. Performance

- asynchronous list loading;
- Room + Flow for reactivity;
- Paging for large lists;
- cache featured-note values with many folders;
- efficient budget recalculation: recompute only the affected branch and its
  ancestors.

---

## 15. Testing

### 15.1 Unit tests

**Folders and notes:**

- creating the first folder;
- cannot create a note without a folder;
- moving a note between folders;
- deleting a folder with confirmation;
- last folder deletion is forbidden;
- notes are not lost when a non-last folder is deleted;
- featured note is cleared when a note is deleted/moved.

**Budget:**

- leaf, group, nested-group recalculation;
- adding/removing/moving a node;
- negative remainder;
- root total;
- collapsing does not change values.

**Reminders:**

- scheduling a one-time reminder;
- cancelling;
- editing;
- restore after reboot;
- reaction to time/timezone change;
- confirmation;
- repeat until confirmed.

**Security:**

- password is hashed;
- pattern is hashed;
- biometrics require fallback;
- secrets do not leak into logs.

**Export/import:**

- full DB export produces a valid file;
- import restores data;
- export contains no secrets;
- imported note is created in the correct folder;
- round-trip: export → import = original data;
- ID conflicts are handled.

### 15.2 UI tests

- first launch;
- creating the first folder;
- app lock;
- creating a note;
- pin, favorite, bold title;
- setting a reminder;
- editing a folder;
- choosing a featured note;
- export/import.

---

## 16. Implementation order

### Phase 0. Clarify blocking questions

Ask open questions one at a time, record answers, update `AGENT_EN.md`.
(`AGENT_RU.md` does **not** exist in the repository; create it only if the user
asks for it.)

### Phase 1. Project skeleton

First verify the build toolchain (JDK, Gradle, Android SDK, emulator/device) —
its availability has not been checked yet (gap G07). Then: Gradle,
modules/packages, Compose, Navigation, DI, Room, DataStore, themes, basic
localization, `.gitignore`. Exit criterion: an empty app builds and launches.

### Phase 2. Folders and notes

First-folder onboarding, folder CRUD, note CRUD, mandatory folder binding,
moving, note properties, main list.

### Phase 3. Budget note

Node model, tree, leaves and groups, cascading recalculation, collapse/expand,
cards, root total.

### Phase 4. Reminders

Model, AlarmManager, channels, sound, vibration, popup, confirmation, restore
after reboot, cancel/edit.

### Phase 5. Security

Password, 3×3 pattern, login/password, biometrics, fallback, secure storage,
lock screen.

### Phase 6. Folder featured note

Featured note selection, metrics, layouts, auto-update of the folder card,
link cleanup.

### Phase 7. Themes, language, settings

Themes, dark theme, folder styles, note colors, RU/EN/FR/DE localization, help
and glossary.

### Phase 8. LIST / TEXT / DRAWING editors

List item model, units, list screen, Markdown editor, raster canvas, drawing
save/load, drawing export.

### Phase 9. Tags and search

Tag model, assignment, search across notes and content, search and grouping by
tags.

### Phase 10. Trash

Soft delete, trash screen, restore, retention cleanup.

### Phase 11. Export and import

Format model, full DB export via SAF, import with confirmation, individual
note export (TEXT / DRAWING / LIST / BUDGET), two modes for LIST and BUDGET,
individual note import with type autodetection, ID conflict handling, cache
recalculation after import, round-trip tests.

### Phase 12. Polish

Empty states, error handling, accessibility, performance, migrations, test
coverage.

---

## 17. Definition of Done

A task is considered done if:

1. The required behavior is implemented.
2. No hardcoded user-facing strings.
3. Light and dark themes are supported where applicable.
4. Data invariants are respected.
5. Tests are added or updated.
6. DB migrations are added if the schema changed.
7. Dangerous actions have confirmation.
8. No secret leaks into logs.
9. UI is accessible to TalkBack.
10. Empty and error states are handled.
11. `AGENT_EN.md` is updated: new decisions in the Decision Log, and the task
    ticked in §21 with a line in §22 (rule 1.1 / 10).

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Q40 | App name | Final name — NoteBudget or something else? | ACCEPTED (D76, by default) | NoteBudget as the working name |

If there are no more open questions, the agent proceeds to Phase 1.

### 18.1 Known gaps and inconsistencies in the specification

Found during the review of agent-1.1. None of these may be "solved" by the agent
on its own: when the phase named in the last column is about to start, the
agent asks the user **one** question about it (recommended option included) and
records the answer in the Decision Log.

| ID | Gap | Must be resolved before |
| --- | --- | --- |
| G01 | Data model/schema is incomplete. **Resolved so far:** soft delete (D81), drawing storage (D83), audio storage (D88), app settings keys (D89), trash retention (D93). **Still open:** tags (D44), list items and units (D37, D69, §6.7), budget operation log (D36), budget periods (D33, D68), currency (D32 — global or per budget note?), repeating reminders (D59 — `ReminderType` has only `ONE_TIME`, no recurrence fields), local user accounts and per-note access grants (D31, D66). | The phase that owns the feature (3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10) |
| G02 | E-mail password recovery (D27, D64) needs a server/network mechanism, but D16 / D75 say there is no server/cloud. Q46 is marked ACCEPTED without defining the mechanism. | Phase 5. **RESOLVED 2026-09-20 (D129):** the user chose their own SMTP server (only the recovery mail leaves the phone, permission `INTERNET`) plus an authenticator app and the security question. |
| G03 | Roles (D31, D66) — admin / read-only user with local accounts — are not reconciled with `AppLockSettings`, which models a single credential set. | Phase 5 |
| G04 | Folder auto-sum of budget notes (D41): **dropped by the user on 2026-09-21 (D151).** | Closed |
| G05 | `FeaturedLayout` has `TITLE_ONLY`, `COMPACT`, `FULL`; §6.4 mentions only `COMPACT` / `FULL`. | Phase 6 |
| G06 | ~~Deletion is required to go through the trash (D29), but the trash is Phase 10 while note/folder deletion appears in Phase 2.~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-19 → D81** (soft delete from the start). | — |
| G07 | Build environment is unverified: JDK, Android SDK, Gradle, emulator/device availability and how the APK will be built and checked in this workspace are unknown. | Phase 1 |
| G08 | Permissions `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` and `USE_EXACT_ALARM` are both listed; `USE_EXACT_ALARM` is restricted by Google Play policy to alarm/calendar apps and cannot be requested by every app. | Phase 4 |
| G09 | ~~The backup format (ZIP + JSON) has no Decision Log entry; the JSON schema and versioning are undefined.~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-20 → D84, D85.** | — |

  fails on it with a bare "25.0.3" error. Build with JDK 17–21, e.g. set
  `JAVA_HOME` to a JDK 21 (the agent used a portable Temurin 21 unpacked outside
  the project) or set Android Studio's Gradle JDK to 21. **Emulator/device: not
  possible on this computer.** It is a VMware VM (`VMware20,1`) whose host does not
  expose virtualization (`emulator -accel-check` → "Virtualization extension is not
  supported"); the SDK also lacks `cmdline-tools`, and the only AVD is Android TV
  (API 34) with no system image installed. Options for a real launch: enable nested
  virtualization for the VM on the VMware host, or use a physical phone over USB, or
  run the project on another machine.*
- [x] `AGENT_EN.md` reviewed and corrected (agent-1.1).

### 21.1 Phase overview

| Phase | Name | Status | Verified by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Clarify blocking questions | DONE | Q40 closed as D76; gaps listed in §18.1 |
| 1 | Project skeleton | IN_PROGRESS | `assembleDebug` succeeds (2026-09-20 15:17, version 0.2.0, APK 34.5 MB). Launched on a phone by the user on 2026-09-20 (first APK); not by the agent |
| 2 | Folders and notes | IN_PROGRESS | domain/data + UI written, builds, unit + Robolectric tests pass; **never run on a device by the agent**; TEXT, LIST, DRAWING, AUDIO editors exist (autosave); no sorting options, no search |
| 3 | Budget note | IN_PROGRESS | cascade logic + editor + tree UI (add/edit/delete/collapse) + tests pass; **never run on a device**; G01 items (periods, currency, log) not done |
| 4 | Reminders | IN_PROGRESS | logic + unit tests pass; alarm/receivers compile, **untested on a device**; no UI |
| 5 | Security | IN_PROGRESS | only `CredentialHasher` (+ tests); lock storage/screens/biometrics not done |
| 6 | Folder featured note | IN_PROGRESS | `setFeatured` rules + tests pass; no UI, no folder-card display |
| 7 | Themes, language, settings | IN_PROGRESS | language, light/dark theme, notification privacy, help + glossary done (EN/RU/FR/DE); folder styles per theme, note colours, density not done; **never run on a device** |
| 8 | LIST / TEXT / DRAWING editors | IN_PROGRESS | TEXT (Markdown toolbar + preview), LIST (comment, quantity, unit, unit totals; units setting; D96), DRAWING (raster + vector, D90), AUDIO (record/play) written and unit-tested; **never run on a device** |
| 9 | Tags and search | NOT_STARTED | — |
| 10 | Trash | IN_PROGRESS | trash screen, restore, delete forever, empty trash, retention setting, purge at app start (D93); 250 JVM tests incl. real Room cascades; **never run on a device** |
| 11 | Export and import | IN_PROGRESS | full backup + single-note export/import written, 37 unit tests incl. golden-file compatibility; **SAF flows never run on a device**; no round trip through real Room (Fakes) |
| 12 | Polish | NOT_STARTED | — |

### 21.3 Summary: done, left, unfinished (audit of 2026-09-20)

Known limits: videos over 40 MB are not put into a backup; the screen recording keeps the screen size of its start; overlay settings are not in backups; the phone's own sound needs Android 10; some apps forbid capture of their sound.
